| untilsub | 0.68s | 1.48x | 1.56x | **1.39x** | 1.40x |
| sincesub3 | 0.66s | 1.91x | 1.31x | 1.20x | **1.14x** |

**The unboxing is worth about 6%, and it is worth it everywhere.** Comparing "dense (was)"
against "dense" row by row, the geometric mean of the sixteen ratios is 1.059 on the medians and
1.055 on the minima, and no row is behind by more than 3% on either. The rows it helps most are
the Until (1.14x), the sub-frame Since (1.16x), the sub-frame Until (1.13x) and the wide Until
(1.08x); the one it cannot help is `P2`, whose left operand is closed, so its structure holds a
count per tuple and a union rather than tables of tuples.

| row | was (s) | now (s) | median | was range | now range |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|
| `sincesub` | 1.69 | **1.47** | 1.16x | 1.32--1.82 | 1.24--1.50 |
| `until` | 1.24 | **1.09** | 1.14x | 1.04--1.27 | 0.88--1.11 |
| `untilsub` | 1.06 | **0.94** | 1.13x | 0.84--1.10 | 0.76--0.98 |
| `sincesub3` | 0.87 | **0.80** | 1.09x | 0.68--0.88 | 0.68--0.83 |
| `untilwide` | 3.08 | **2.84** | 1.08x | 2.63--3.24 | 2.45--2.94 |
| `agg` | 0.92 | **0.85** | 1.08x | 0.78--0.94 | 0.70--0.87 |
| `aggwide` | 9.27 | **8.92** | 1.04x | 8.52--10.17 | 8.00--9.46 |

The per-cell spread on this container runs from 11% to 33% of the median (21% at the median), so
a single row's 6% is not readable on its own; sixteen rows all leaning the same way are. The
minima, which are the cleanest estimator on a machine whose noise can only add time, agree: 1.055
against 1.059.

**The dense monitor is at or ahead of MonPoly on seven of the sixteen rows** -- `and` and
`complex` with the heap index, `andnot`, `once`, `eventually`, `sincewide`, `untilwide` and
`aggwide` -- and 20.35's split is unchanged: short tuples pay where there are columns to shorten.
Against the pure verified kernel the dense one is ahead on every row but `until` and `untilsub`,
where the deque's own cost dominates.

**The heap index has stopped earning its keep.** `-heap_maps` was the best configuration on eight
of sixteen rows in 20.35 and is the best on six here, but its margin over plain `-dense` is now
under 2% on every row except `and` (0.94x against 1.03x) and `sincesub3` (1.14x against 1.20x):
the tree it was avoiding got cheaper, because its comparator is now `edl_comparator` over the
short tuples rather than the composite one over the wide. That is worth remembering the next time
the flag's default is considered.

**What is left.** `MAndTrigger` and the two `MatchP`/`MatchF` operators still fall back to the
evaluation function over the wide tuples (20.21), which is also the only thing that still converts
a database between the two representations. 20.38 to 20.43 close that: every constructor gets a
dense node, the fallback node loses its payload, and the export list drops the wide evaluator
altogether.

### 20.38 A node that widens its operands is not a dense node

The three operators 20.37 left over -- the trigger, the two match operators, and the Since and
the Until whose shape the compiler's guard rejects -- all have the same structure in the kernel:
a subtree that is easy to compile, and an auxiliary structure whose operations are stated over
wide tuples. The first attempt took the cheap way out and gave each of them a node that keeps
the kernel's structure inside and converts at the boundary: widen the operands coming up from
the dense children, run `update_mmtaux`/`update_matchP`/`add_new_table_mmsaux` unchanged, narrow
the result on the way out.

It worked, it removed every fallback node from the corpus, and it was wrong for the purpose.
`grep -c '(event_data option) list' src/verified.ml` did not move, because the wide operations
were still there and still reachable; the tuples were being unboxed by the children and boxed
again by the parent, once per tuple per time point, and the only thing the exercise bought was a
smaller count of nodes labelled "fallback". The right reading of that count is the one the code
gives, not the one the label gives.

So the three nodes were reverted (`d6e8b48`) and rebuilt on structures of their own. What
follows is the three pieces: the aggregation the Since and the Until carry (20.39), the trigger
(20.40), the two match operators (20.41), then the conjunction with a trigger and the removal of
the fallback (20.42), the measurements (20.43), and what is left of the wide tuple type (20.44).

### 20.39 The aggregation, kept fused

`meinit0` fuses an aggregation over a temporal operator into the operator: `Agg (Since φ I ψ)`
becomes a single `MSince` whose `args_agg` is `Some aggargs`, and `result_mmasaux` applies the
aggregation to the window's table instead of returning it -- incrementally, off a structure that
the window's insertions and deletions keep up to date, falling back to `eval_aggargs` over the
whole window only where no such structure exists for the aggregation in question.

The dense compiler could not place such a node, because the columns it produces are the
aggregation's group-by columns plus the result variable, not `args_R args`, and the guard
`args_R args = set cs` fails.

**The first attempt un-fused it**, compiling the node as a dense `DAgg` over a dense `DSince`:
`dagg_fused` recognised the shape, `dunfuse` dropped the aggregation from the arguments, and
`dwrap_agg` put an aggregation node back on top. Both halves were then ordinary dense nodes with
no new structure and no new trust -- and the aggregate was recomputed from the whole window at
every time point, because the dense layer had nothing to maintain it with. On
`z <- CNT x ONCE[0,10] C(x)` that cost 1.41x against falling back to the wide evaluator and 1.76x
against the wide kernel itself. It was a substitution of a cheaper thing for the thing asked for,
and the measurement is what made it visible.

**So the structure exists.** `Optimized_Agg`'s is a mapping from the group tuple to what the
aggregation needs -- a count, a count and a sum, or the sorted multiset of the values for the
rank aggregations -- and only three things in it read a tuple:

- the group key, `drop (length tys) t`, which over short tuples is the same `drop`, because
  safety makes the bound variables the first columns of the operand's frame;
- the aggregated value, `meval_trm f t`, which over short tuples is `umeval_trm` of the term
  reindexed to the operand's frame -- the same term the non-incremental `uagg` evaluates;
- the result, which writes the aggregate into the group tuple: over wide tuples by overwriting
  the result variable's column, over short ones by inserting it at the position that variable has
  among the node's columns.

Everything else -- the three aggregate kinds, the treelists of the rank aggregations, the types
they are restricted to -- is about the values, not the tuples, and is reused unchanged. What
replaces the parameters is five numbers, `dagg_of`: the two flags of the aggregation, the
reindexed term, how many columns come before the groups, and where the result goes.

**Maintaining it needs the window's deltas.** Three operations of the dense Since move tuples in
or out -- the shift at the window's far end, the move into the window, the join -- and each
already computes the set that moved. They get variants that return it, each with a lemma that
dropping it gives the original operation back:

```isabelle
lemma gshift_end_del_fst: "fst (gshift_end_del args nt s) = gshift_end args nt s"
  by (cases s rule: gmsaux_cases) (simp add: Let_def split_beta)
```

so the aggregating path runs the same body rather than a copy of it. The join's answer is not a
set: where the node has no join columns it either keeps its window or empties it, and emptying it
is a reset of the structure rather than a deletion of known tuples, which `None` says.

The Until's deltas are the front map of its deque. A step drops the time point at the front, which
deletes what expired with it and inserts the map that becomes the front; adding a time point
inserts what it puts at the front, and that is exactly the difference between the two results,
because `result` is the front map's key set -- the kernel computes the same set the long way, as
`to_add`.

Both structures become one more representation of the corresponding operator, picked exactly when
the node carries an aggregation. One deviation from the kernel: it folds its update over the
table with `Finite_Set.fold`, which the code generator needs a `comp_fun_commute` witness for, one
per aggregate kind; folding the sorted list of the table instead needs none and generates the same
loop.

### 20.40 The trigger structure over short tuples

`Optimized_Trigger.thy`'s `mmtaux` is a thirteen-tuple: a timestamp, three indices, the two
masks, the queue of tuples still in the lookahead, the queue of tuples in the window, the
mapping from a tuple to the timestamp at which it last appeared on the left, the set of keys of
that mapping, the mapping from a tuple to the index since which it has held, and the two sets of
tuples that are historically satisfied and since-satisfied respectively. Seven of the thirteen
carry tables or tuple-keyed mappings, and those carry over to short tuples by retyping; two
things in the operations do not.

The first is `proj_tuple_in_join_optim`, the masked semi-join that decides whether a tuple of
the window joins with the left operand's table. Over wide tuples it projects the tuple to the
join columns by overwriting the others with `None`; over short ones the dense layer already has
`dsemi`, which tests membership of the projection under the compile-time mask, so `gsemi_join`
is the same three cases with `dsemi` in place of the projection.

The second is the stored `maskR`. The kernel keeps it to rebuild a right-operand tuple from a
wide one; at the dense frame every column of the node is a column of the right operand, so there
is nothing to rebuild and the field goes away. What is left is `'b gmmtaux` and the four
operations `ginit_mmtaux`, `gupdate_mmtaux'`, `gupdate_mmtaux` and `gresult_mmtaux`, generic in
the element type like the rest of `Dense_Unbox`, and specialised at `event_data` by
`Dense_Code_Opt` with everything else.

`gresult_mmtaux` returns `tuple_in_once_keys ∪ hist_sat ∪ since_sat`, which is the kernel's
result *on a non-empty window*. On an empty one the kernel returns `{replicate n None}` -- "every
valuation" -- which a short tuple cannot express at all. That case is unreachable: the compiler's
guard requires `mem (args_ivl args) 0`, so the current time point is always in the window, and
the window is never empty. The guard is what makes the node sound, and a formula that does not
satisfy it is refused rather than mis-evaluated (20.42).

### 20.41 The match structures over short tuples

The two match structures are lists of tables indexed by a timestamp and, for the past one, by a
marked regular expression, so they carry over on their own. The derivative operations that fill
them do not, in two places.

The first is the join against an atom inside `ε_lax`: the guard is a table over the node's
columns, the atom's table is over a subset of them, and over wide tuples that is `join`. Over
short tuples it is a semi-join against the atom's mask -- and the masks are computed once, at
compile time, one per atom, which is what the `bool list list` field of `DMatchP`/`DMatchF`
holds. Where both sides are results of the lax epsilon, `MTimes` becomes plain intersection,
because both are already at the node's frame.

The second is the unit table of `rε_strict`/`lε_strict` at `Skip 0` and `Star`: over wide tuples
`{replicate n None}`, over short ones `{[]}`, which is the right answer only when the node has no
columns. Safety forces exactly that -- a `Skip 0` or a `Star` contributes no free variable -- so
the two agree wherever the compiler places the node.

One thing had to be added that is not a refinement at all. `mrtabulate` builds a mapping from
marked regexes to tables, and its `RBT_Mapping` code equation is stated for the kernel's own
table type. Without a matching equation for the generic `gmrtabulate` the code generator falls
back to the function-backed `Mapping`, whose `lookup` then aborts at the first `Mapping.lookup`
on an `RBT_Mapping` -- 21 failures in `cov_regex.t`, all with `Pattern matching failed`. The
equation is the one line

```isabelle
lemma gmrtabulate_rbt_code[code]: "gmrtabulate (xs :: mregex list) f =
  (case ID CCOMPARE(mregex) of
     None ⇒ Code.abort (STR ''gmrtabulate RBT_Mapping: ccompare = None'') (λ_. gmrtabulate xs f)
   | _ ⇒ RBT_Mapping (RBT_Mapping2.bulkload
       (List.map_filter (λk. let fk = f k in if fk = {} then None else Some (k, fk)) xs)))"
```

and it is worth recording as a failure mode: a missing code equation on a `Mapping` does not
produce slow code, it produces code that raises.

### 20.42 The conjunction with a trigger, and the end of the fallback

`MAndTrigger` is the kernel's fused node for `φ ∧ (γ TRIGGER[I] β)`: it evaluates the three
operands, runs the trigger structure over the second and the third, and joins the first with the
result. The join is `bin_join n V r1 True fv_z r2`, where `fv_z` is the column set the trigger
structure reports alongside its table -- `{}` when the window is empty, `args_R args` otherwise.

**The first version of this node copied 20.40's `mem I 0` guard, and that was exactly backwards.**
Read `safe_dual`: with `mem I 0` it ignores whether the trigger is conjoined, so a trigger the
`And` case would accept is also accepted standing alone, and `meinit0`'s `safe_formula ψ` branch
fires first and builds a plain `MAnd`. `MAndTrigger` is therefore built *only* when the trigger is
unsafe on its own, which -- given the `And` is safe -- means exactly `¬ mem I 0`. The guard never
fired, and after 20.42 removed the fallback, every `φ AND (γ TRIGGER[1,3] β)` died at
initialisation with *"the formula is outside the dense fragment"*. The whole corpus passed anyway,
because it contains no conjoined trigger at all: `verimon_tests/cov_verified.t` has six `TRIGGER`
formulas and every one of them is standalone. A count of passing invocations is not coverage of a
guard nobody exercises.

So the node handles the empty window instead of excluding it. `safe_dual` at `¬ mem I 0` forces
`fv γ = fv β`, and `safe_formula` for the conjunction forces `fv ψ ⊆ fv φ`, so `args_R ⊆ V` and the
node's columns are `V`: on a non-empty window the join `mk_djoin (set_cols V) (set_cols (args_R
args))` is a semi-join into `V`, and on an empty one the kernel's join with the wildcard tuple is
the identity, so the answer is the left operand unchanged. `gmmtaux_empty` tells the two apart,
the trigger's output list becomes a `utable option` list with `None` for "every valuation", and
the buffer between the two carries the option on both sides so that `mebuf2_take_j` can be used
unchanged. The guard that is left is `args_n args = n`, `cs = merge_cols cs_V cs_R`,
`set cs_R ⊆ set cs_V` and `set cs_L ⊆ set cs_R` -- shape conditions, no interval condition --
and `mcols` reports `merge_cols (set_cols V) (set_cols (args_R args))`, which is what `MAnd`
reports for a positive conjunction.

Six conjoined-trigger formulas are now in `cov_verified.t`, and a sweep of 113 formulas around
every guard in the compiler -- triggers and releases at five intervals, standalone and conjoined,
both polarities, under `EXISTS`, under `ONCE`, under a further conjunct; the match operators; the
aggregations over temporal operators -- agrees with the pre-series kernel on all 113, with no
formula refused.

With that clause in place the compiler's catch-all became redundant -- Isabelle said so, in the
form of *"Equation is redundant (covered by preceding clauses)"* on the last line of
`dformula_of`. Every constructor of the sparse formula now has a dense clause.

So the fallback node loses its payload. `DPure mformula_j nat "bool list"` becomes `DFail`, the
guarded branches return it instead of packing up a sparse subformula, `dpure_nodes` becomes
`dfail_nodes`, and `minit_safe_d` refuses a formula that has one:

```isabelle
definition minit_safe_d :: "ty Formula.formula ⇒ dstate Heap" where
  "minit_safe_d φ = do {
     m ← mformula_j_of (meinit0 (Formula.nfv φ) φ);
     d ← dformula_of (Formula.nfv φ) [0..<Formula.nfv φ] m;
     if dfail_nodes d = 0 then return (0, 0, d, Formula.nfv φ, empty_queue)
     else raise (STR ''minit_safe_d: the formula is outside the dense fragment'') }"
```

This is the substantive change of the series, and it is a change of contract. Before it, a shape
the guards reject was evaluated by the wide function and the user saw correct output, slowly.
After it, such a shape is a loud failure at initialisation. That makes every guard load-bearing,
and the corpus is not enough to check them -- the conjoined trigger above is the proof. The
guards are the shape conditions of 20.14, `mem I 0` for the standalone trigger (which `safe_dual`
guarantees), and for the rest the ones no safe formula reaches: `MAnds` with an empty positive
list, `MExists` over a subtree whose columns are unknown, and a `MatchP`/`MatchF` whose operands'
columns do not union to the node's.

### 20.43 Exporting only the dense monitor

With the fallback unreachable, nothing in the dense evaluator calls the evaluation function over
the wide tuples any more, and the export list can say so. `Monitor_Code_Imp.thy` drops
`minit_safe`/`mstep` (the pure kernel), `vminit_safe`/`vmstep`, `minit_safe_imp`/`mstep_imp` and
`minit_safe_j`/`mstep_j` (the two heap-resident kernels), the wide database operations, and the
hash-table and map primitives that only the micro-benchmarks used. What is left is
`minit_safe_d`, `mstep_d`, `dfail_nodes`, the four operations on the database of short tuples,
and the front end's own view of a formula (`convert_multiway`, `mmonitorable_exec`,
`type_check`).

The code generator does the rest:

| | before | after |
|---|---:|---:|
| `src/verified.ml`, lines | 20390 | **10530** |
| occurrences of `(event_data option) list` | 362 | **71** |
| evaluation functions over wide tuples | 3 | **0** |

`meval`, `meeval`, `meval_imp`, `meval_j`, `bin_join`, `mmulti_join`, `proj_tuple`, the wide
`mmsaux`/`mmuaux`/`mmtaux` updates, the wide match derivatives and `eval_matchF` are all gone.
What the remaining occurrences of the wide tuple type are, and which of them can go, is 20.44.

The adapter loses its dispatch with the same stroke: one database type instead of
`Wide | Short`, one batch, one state, one verdict type, and `step` is a single call to `mstep_d`.
`-dense`, `-heap_maps` and `-heap_since` are accepted and ignored, so existing command lines keep
working; the monitor they used to select is now the only one.

**The suite.** Seven interleaved repetitions, medians, multipliers relative to MonPoly where
MonPoly can monitor the formula, lower is better. "verimon (was)" and "dense (was)" are the
kernel this series started from, run as `-verified` and `-verified -dense`; "verimon" is the
kernel after it, where `-verified` *is* the dense monitor. The five rows below the rule are the
ones that used to fall back: `trigger`, `andtrigger`, `matchp` and `frz*` were evaluated by the
wide function even under `-dense`, and `aggonce` is the aggregating temporal operator of 20.39,
which the old `-dense` also fell back for. `frz*` runs on a 400-time-point log because the freeze
operator is quadratic in the log on all three kernels.

| formula | MonPoly | verimon (was) | dense (was) | verimon |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `P2` | 0.49s | 2.57x | 1.76x | **1.64x** |
| `P3` | 0.53s | 2.20x | 1.48x | **1.46x** |
| `and` | 0.73s | 1.47x | 1.02x | **1.00x** |
| `andnot` | 0.74s | 0.93x | **0.89x** | 0.89x |
| `once` | 3.38s | 1.12x | 0.73x | **0.73x** |
| `since` | 1.15s | 1.26x | **1.17x** | 1.18x |
| `eventually` | 2.21s | 1.30x | 0.82x | **0.81x** |
| `until` | 1.08s | 1.48x | **1.38x** | 1.41x |
| `agg` | 0.79s | 1.57x | 1.45x | **1.44x** |
| `complex` | 3.99s | 1.82x | 1.02x | **1.01x** |
| `sincewide` | 4.36s | 1.05x | **0.86x** | 0.87x |
| `untilwide` | 4.30s | 1.10x | **0.90x** | 0.90x |
| `aggwide` | 17.04s | 1.00x | **0.75x** | 0.76x |
| `sincesub` | 1.06s | 2.65x | **1.94x** | 1.94x |
| `untilsub` | 0.91s | 1.54x | **1.43x** | 1.48x |
| `sincesub3` | 0.88s | 1.98x | 1.24x | **1.22x** |
| `trigger` | -- | 0.92s | 1.13s | **0.84s** |
| `andtrigger` | -- | 0.98s | 1.18s | **0.89s** |
| `matchp` | -- | 0.64s | 0.88s | **0.61s** |
| `frz*` | -- | 1.32s | 1.49s | **1.09s** |
| `aggonce` | 0.42s | 2.08x | 2.56x | **1.89x** |

The per-cell spread is 7% of the median (4% to 12%), a good deal tighter than 20.37's 21%; the
container is slower than it was there, so the absolute seconds are not comparable across sections
and the multipliers within a row are.

**On the sixteen rows that were already dense, removing the wide evaluator changes nothing.** The
geometric mean of `dense (was)` over `verimon` is 1.005 on the medians and 1.007 on the minima,
and no row moves by more than 4%. That is the expected answer and the reassuring one: those rows
never entered the fallback, so deleting it could only have hurt. What this series buys on them is
not time, it is the 9000 lines of kernel that no longer exist.

**On the five rows that used to fall back, it is worth 1.37x.**

| row | wide (was) | dense (was) | verimon | against `dense (was)` |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `matchp` | 0.64s | 0.88s | **0.61s** | 1.44x |
| `aggonce` | 0.87s | 1.07s | **0.79s** | 1.36x |
| `frz*` | 1.32s | 1.49s | **1.09s** | 1.37x |
| `trigger` | 0.92s | 1.13s | **0.84s** | 1.35x |
| `andtrigger` | 0.98s | 1.18s | **0.89s** | 1.33x |

Note the middle column: under `-dense`, these five were *slower* than the plain wide kernel, by
20% to 38%. That is the fallback's own cost -- the database widened for the node, its result
narrowed again, once per time point -- and it is what 20.38 said a widening node buys you. With
the nodes dense throughout, all five are ahead of the wide kernel as well (1.11x geometric mean
over the four operator rows, 1.10x on `aggonce`), which is the first time the trigger, the match
operators and the aggregating temporal operators have been.

`aggonce` is the row this series got wrong once and then right: 3.54x MonPoly with the
aggregation un-fused (20.39), 1.89x with it fused and maintained at the dense frame, against
2.08x for the wide kernel and 2.56x for the old fallback.

**Against MonPoly the split is 20.35's, unchanged.** The dense monitor is at or ahead on six of
the sixteen comparable rows (`andnot`, `once`, `eventually`, `sincewide`, `untilwide`, `aggwide`,
and level on `and` and `complex`), and behind where there are no empty columns to drop --
`P2`/`P3`, whose left operands are closed, and the sub-frame Since. The rows MonPoly cannot
monitor at all are now dense throughout.

**What is left.** The freeze operator is quadratic in the log on every kernel -- 400 time points
take a second, 8000 take more than ten minutes -- which is an algorithmic problem in
`freeze_meval0`, not a representation one, and is untouched by any of this. And the dense
evaluator is still unverified: `Dense_Imp` is the executable layer, and the simulation theorem of
20.9 covers the pure dense algorithm, not `deval`.

### 20.44 What is left of the wide tuple type

After 20.43 the generated kernel had 71 occurrences of `(event_data option) list`, all of them in
declarations rather than in anything the monitor evaluates. They fall into four groups:

| where | occurrences | what it is |
|---|---:|---|
| `meformula` | 24 | the sparse formula's own fields: `MRel`'s table, the buffers of the binary and temporal nodes, the tables of the let and the freeze |
| `msaux_j`, `muaux_j` | 24 | the auxiliary structures of an *intermediate* formula the compiler descended |
| `muaux_dq`, `aggaux` | 13 | the kernel's Until deque and aggregation structures, which `meinit0` fills the sparse formula with |
| `ed_bot`, `ed_mempty_*`, ... | 10 | the empty values those initialisations need |

**The second group was removable, and is gone.** The compiler read exactly one thing out of the
sparse formula's auxiliary fields: whether a Since node's structure was the heap-resident one,
because a subtree the freeze node replays cannot keep anything on the heap. It read it off an
intermediate formula built for the purpose (`mformula_j_of`), which allocated a Since structure at
every such node only for the compiler to discard it and allocate its own.

But that condition is the compiler's own. `mformula_j_of` puts a subtree into the pure
representation exactly under the let, the recursive let, the freeze and the two match operators --
its explicit clauses cover everything else, and its catch-all is `embed`. So the compiler carries
a flag instead: true at the root, false under those five constructors, and passed to `init_d` in
place of the field it used to read. `dformula_of` now descends the formula `meinit0` builds, and
`mformula_j`, `msaux_j`, `muaux_j`, `embed`, `purify`, `init_j` and `init_u_j` are gone from the
generated code along with the double allocation. `src/verified.ml` loses another 274 lines and
the count goes 71 → 47.

**The other three groups are not removable without changing the verified kernel.** They are all
`meinit0`'s doing: it builds the sparse formula, and that formula's type is the kernel's, with
tables in its own fields and the kernel's auxiliary structures in its parameters. Two ways out
were considered and rejected:

- *Descend the formula rather than the sparse formula.* A `dinit0 :: ty formula ⇒ dformula` would
  never mention `meformula` at all -- and would duplicate `meinit0`'s dispatch, including which
  node shape each safe formula gets and the rewrites that precede it. That dispatch is precisely
  what the dense compiler exists to follow rather than to re-decide; duplicating it would make the
  dense monitor a second compiler that has to be kept in step with the kernel's by hand.
- *Instantiate `meinit0` at a trivial auxiliary type.* `meinit0` is defined `(in maux)`, and
  `maux` is a locale whose assumptions say the structures represent their windows. `unit` does not
  satisfy them, so there is no interpretation to instantiate at, and the in-locale equations are
  not available for code generation outside one.

What the remaining 47 cost at run time is one empty structure per node at initialisation, read
never again: no wide tuple is constructed, compared, hashed or stored while the monitor runs. The
count is a useful proxy for "is the wide representation still in the loop", and it has done its
job; past this point it measures the kernel's own formula type, not the monitor's data.

### 20.45 The verified dense evaluator, measured

20.43 ended by saying the shipping dense monitor is not the one the simulation theorem is about:
`Dense_Imp.deval` is the executable layer, and `dsim_deval` covers `Dense_Eval.deval`, the pure
dense algorithm, which nothing had ever run. This section runs it.

**What had to be built.** `Dense_Eval.deval` is a `function (sequential)` over a `dformula` whose
escape hatch carries a kernel formula, so exporting it drags the wide kernel back in; and
`Dense_Descend.dof`, the verified compiler, pattern-matches on `mformula` constructors, which are
not code constructors (`code_datatype Rep_meformula`). Two pieces closed the gap:

- `Dense_Descend_Code.thy`, an `edof` over `meformula` that mirrors `dof` clause for clause, with
  `edof_dof: edof n cs e = dof n cs (Rep_meformula e)` and `dof_code[code]` the other way round.
  Its fallbacks have to be written `DPure (Rep_meformula (meformula.MTrigger …)) n msk` rather
  than `DPure (mformula.MTrigger …) n msk` -- *constructing* a kernel formula has no code
  equation. That is the sharpest statement of what `DPure` costs: it is the one place where the
  verified dense fragment still needs the kernel's formula type as data.
- `mstate_sel_code[code]` for the five `mstate` selectors against `mestate`, since `dof_state`
  reads them and the record constructor is likewise not a code constructor.

With `dminit_safe φ = dof_state (nfv φ) (set_cols (fv φ)) (minit_safe φ)`, `dmstep` and
`vdpure_nodes` exported, and the adapter's wide path restored behind a `-vdense` flag, the binary
runs three evaluators: `-verified` (shipping dense), `-verified -vdense` (the verified dense
algorithm, wide tuples, dense columns), and `monpoly_was -verified` (the wide kernel from before
the series, at `431a1da`).

**Coverage and correctness first.** `vdpure_nodes` reports how many subformulas the compiler left
to the kernel's evaluator. On 19 of the 21 benchmark formulas it is 0 -- the verified dense
algorithm evaluates the whole formula. The two that are not are `matchp` (the verified fragment
has no `DMatchP`) and `aggonce` (a fused aggregation; `dof` requires `args_agg args = None`), each
with one fallback node. All 37 monitorable formulas of `cov_verified.t` give identical verdicts
under `-verified` and `-verified -vdense`.

**The measurement.** Seven interleaved repetitions, median reported, spread (max-min)/median 6%
median and 20% worst.

| formula | MonPoly | wide kernel | verified dense | shipping dense |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| `P2` | 0.50s | 2.59x | 205.34x | 1.66x |
| `P3` | 0.53s | 2.19x | 2.42x | 1.42x |
| `and` | 0.76s | 1.39x | 0.99x | 0.97x |
| `andnot` | 0.77s | 0.91x | 0.89x | 0.89x |
| `once` | 3.46s | 1.10x | 1.10x | 0.73x |
| `since` | 1.22s | 1.21x | 1.21x | 1.13x |
| `eventually` | 2.29s | 1.23x | 1.34x | 0.81x |
| `until` | 1.12s | 1.42x | 1.53x | 1.36x |
| `agg` | 0.82s | 1.49x | 1.43x | 1.40x |
| `complex` | 4.09s | 1.74x | 1.34x | 0.97x |
| `sincewide` | 4.45s | 1.02x | 0.88x | 0.84x |
| `untilwide` | 4.43s | 1.07x | 0.93x | 0.88x |
| `aggwide` | 17.58s | 0.97x | 0.75x | 0.72x |
| `sincesub` | 1.10s | 2.52x | 2.06x | 1.85x |
| `untilsub` | 0.96s | 1.44x | 1.49x | 1.39x |
| `sincesub3` | 0.92s | 1.88x | 1.24x | 1.20x |
| `trigger` | -- | 0.91s | 1.02s | 0.87s |
| `andtrigger` | -- | 0.95s | 0.95s | 0.89s |
| `matchp` | -- | 0.65s | 0.64s | 0.63s |
| `frz*` | -- | 1.28s | 1.30s | 1.05s |
| `aggonce` | 0.44s | 1.99x | 2.00x | 1.82x |

Over the twenty rows other than `P2` -- see below for why `P2` is excluded rather than averaged --
the geometric means split the series in two:

| step | what it is | factor |
|---|---|---:|
| wide kernel -> verified dense | the dense columns alone, on wide tuples, in pure HOL | 0.931 |
| verified dense -> shipping dense | unboxing, the heap structures, the operator specialisations | 0.865 |
| wide kernel -> shipping dense | both | 0.805 |

**So the columns are worth 7%, and everything after them 14%.** That is the first honest split of
the series, and it is not the split 20.37 guessed at: the dense columns, which is all the verified
algorithm does, are the smaller half. The reason is visible row by row. Where the formula has
empty columns to drop the verified algorithm already gets most of the win (`aggwide` 0.97 -> 0.75,
`sincesub3` 1.88 -> 1.24, `complex` 1.74 -> 1.34, `and` 1.39 -> 0.99); where it does not, it is
level with the wide kernel or slightly behind, because it still allocates an `event_data option
list` per tuple and still runs the pure deque structures. The shipping monitor's extra 14% is
almost entirely the short tuple and the heap-resident Since -- it is a uniform small factor on
nearly every row rather than a large factor on a few.

**`P2` is a real pathology, not noise.** 1.31s in the wide kernel, 103.5s in the verified dense
algorithm. Bisecting the formula:

| subformula | wide kernel | verified dense |
|---|---:|---:|
| `trans(c,t,a) AND 2000 < a` | 0.39s | 0.33s |
| `EVENTUALLY[0,6) report(t)` | 17.06s | 73.15s |
| `trans(c,t,a) AND NOT EVENTUALLY[0,6) report(t)` | 1.08s | 105.67s |

The `EVENTUALLY` node is the whole of it, and the cause is 20.34's specialisation. `evt_applies
args = (args_agg args = None ∧ args_L args = {} ∧ memL (args_ivl args) 0)` -- a closed left operand
and an interval containing 0, which is exactly `EVENTUALLY` -- selects a structure that answers
"does some later time point in the window satisfy the operand" with a running suffix union, linear
in the number of pending time points. Without it the general Until updates every pending time
point's table at every step, which is quadratic in the number of time points inside the window.
`dof` has no such case, so the verified dense algorithm always takes the general path.

Whether that is visible depends entirely on the log, which is why `eventually` on `syn.log` shows
nothing (2.83s -> 3.07s) while `P2` shows a factor of 79. `syn.log` has 8000 time points and 8000
distinct timestamps, so `[0,10]` holds about ten time points at once. `P2.log` has 201157 time
points and **106** distinct timestamps -- about 1900 events per timestamp, each its own time point
-- so `[0,6)` holds thousands, and the quadratic term is the whole run. The wide kernel's 17.06s
on the subformula alone, against 1.08s inside the conjunction, is verdict printing: as a top-level
formula it reports every satisfying `t` at every time point.

This is worth recording as a general point about the benchmark suite. A specialisation whose
benefit is quadratic in the window's time-point count is invisible on a log with one time point
per timestamp, and dominant on a log without. `syn.log` is the former; the `P2`/`P3` logs are the
latter, and they are the realistic shape.

**What this settles.** The verified dense algorithm runs, on the whole of nineteen of twenty-one
benchmark formulas, at 0.93x the wide kernel it is proved to simulate. The 0.87x between it and
the shipping monitor is the gap the verification has yet to cross, and the `P2` row says what the
first item in it is: not the representation, but the operator specialisations that the compiler
chooses and `dof` does not.

### 20.46 `DPure` removed, and the list recursions folded into `map`

20.45 built the verified dense evaluator a second time in order to measure it, and in doing so
named the one thing that still tied it to the kernel: `DPure`. This section removes it, and
replaces the hand-written list recursions by `map`.

**What `DPure` was.** One constructor of `dformula` carrying a kernel formula:

```isabelle
datatype dformula = DPure "(mmasaux, muaux_dq, event_data mmtaux) mformula" nat "bool list" | ...
| "deval j ts db (DPure φ m msk) =
     (let (xs, φ') = meval j m ts db φ in (map (dnarrow msk) xs, DPure φ' m msk))"
```

It made `dof` total on the kernel's formula language and `dsim_dof_minit0` unconditional: a node
the dense fragment did not cover was related to the kernel by `DsPure` and evaluated by the
kernel's own `meval`. That is why exporting `deval` dragged in the whole wide-tuple evaluator,
and why 20.45's `Dense_Descend_Code.thy` had to write its fallbacks as `DPure (Rep_meformula
(meformula.MTrigger …)) …` -- the generated code cannot *construct* a value of the specification
type.

**What replaces it.** A nullary `DFail`, the same shape the executable evaluator has used since
20.42, with `deval j ts db DFail = ([], DFail)` and no `dsim` rule; `vdfail_nodes` counts them.
Nothing in `dformula` mentions `mformula` any more, and `deval` calls nothing of the kernel's
evaluator. In the generated code `meeval` now occurs zero times.

**The price is one hypothesis, in one place.** Every statement of `Dense_Descend.thy` that read
`dofc n tw φ` now reads `dofz n φ ⟹ dofc n tw φ`, where

```isabelle
dofz n φ ≡ vdfail_nodes (dof n (set_cols (Formula.fv φ)) (minit0 n φ)) = 0
```

and each per-constructor lemma splits the parent's `dofz` into its operands' with the node's own
compilation equation -- one `simp` each, because `vdfail_nodes` adds. A constructor the fragment
does not cover no longer needs a lemma at all: its case is vacuous and `dsim_dof_minit0_fail` is
`by simp`. `dsim_dof_pure`, `dsim_dof_minit0_pure`, `dpure_state`, `dmonitor_mverdicts_pure` and
`dmonitor_mverdicts_dense` are gone. What remains is `dmonitor_mverdicts_dof`, with

```isabelle
vdfail_nodes (dmstate_m (dof_state (Formula.nfv φ) (set_cols (Formula.fv φ)) (minit_safe φ))) = 0
```

as its one added assumption. The front end checks it once, before the first time point, and
**refuses the formula** when it fails -- with no fallback left, a `DFail` node would report
nothing and say nothing about it.

**This is the honest statement of what the verified monitor covers**, and it is the statement the
executable one has made since 20.42. Before, a formula outside the fragment was monitored
correctly by a *different* algorithm and the theorem said nothing about which one had run. Now
the theorem covers the whole run, or the run does not start. Concretely, `-verified -vdense` now
rejects the two benchmark formulas 20.45 reported a fallback node for: `matchp` (no `DMatchP` in
the verified fragment) and `aggonce` (a fused aggregation). Those two rows of 20.45's table can
no longer be produced; the other nineteen are unaffected, because the algorithm that ran them is
unchanged.

**The list recursions.** `deval` was defined mutually with `deval_list`, `dof` with `dof_list`
and `edof` with `edof_list`, each a hand-written map:

```isabelle
  "deval_list j ts db [] = []"
| "deval_list j ts db (φ # r) = deval j ts db φ # deval_list j ts db r"
```

`map_cong` is a `fundef_cong` rule, so the mutual definitions bought nothing: `map (deval j ts
db) Ld` and `map2 (dof n) (c0 # css @ nss) L` are accepted directly. For `deval` the termination
argument gets *simpler* -- one measure `size φ` instead of a sum type, and `psize_snd_deval`
loses its second conjunct and the `size_list_map_cong` lemma that carried it. `deval_list_rel`
and the `DAnds` case of `dsim_deval` needed no proof change at all: `list.map`'s simp rules are
the ones `deval_list.simps` used to be. `length_dof_list` and `dof_list_nth` become `min` (which
the simplifier knows) and a three-line `map2_nth`.

**Two things `map2` costs that `map` does not.** `fun`'s automatic termination proof cannot see
through `map2 (dof n) css L`: the congruence hands it `x ∈ set (zip css L)` and it has no way
from there to `snd x ∈ set L`. So `dof` and `edof` become explicit function definitions:

```isabelle
termination dof
  by (relation "measure (λ(n, cs, φ). size φ)")
    (auto simp: termination_simp dest!: set_zip_rightD)
```

And `edof_dof`'s `DAnds` case needs `map2 (edof n) css L = map2 (dof n) css (map Rep_meformula
L)`, which is not a `map_cong` away because the two `zip`s differ. `zip_map2` brings them to the
same shape -- `zip xs (map h ys) = map (λ(x, y). (x, h y)) (zip xs ys)` -- and then `map_cong`
reduces the goal to the induction hypothesis, one element at a time.

**One trap, worth recording.** `fv_remove_neg` is stated `fv φ = fv (remove_neg φ)`, so adding it
to a simp set rewrites in the expanding direction and does not terminate; every use needs
`[symmetric]`. Under PIDE this presents as a command that stays "running" with a tiny elapsed
time and an `Interrupt_Breakdown` that moves to a different command on each poll -- easy to read
as a stale marker rather than as a loop. The batch build says what it is in one line: `Warning -
Unable to increase stack - interrupting thread`.

**What it costs and what it buys.** `MFODL_Monitor_Imp` builds in 5:37 and the cram suite passes.
Of the twenty-two formulas checked differentially, twenty-one give identical verdicts under
`-verified` and `-verified -vdense` and one (`aggonce`) is refused; none differ.
`src/verified.ml` is 13679 lines against the 19299 of 20.45's measurement export, and 11415 for
the shipping monitor alone: the 2264-line difference is the verified dense evaluator and the wide
*tables* it holds, not the wide *evaluator*, which is gone. The 143 occurrences of `(event_data
option) list`, against 46 in the shipping-only export, are that evaluator's tuple type -- which
is the point of 20.45's measurement and the reason it is still exported.

**The rest of them.** Four more mutual definitions of that shape were left elsewhere in the
session: `dfail_nodes`/`dfail_nodes_list` and `dformula_of`/`dformula_of_list` in `Dense_Imp`,
and `meval_j`/`meval_j_list` and `mformula_j_of`/`mformula_j_of_list` in `Monitor_Imp2`. The
first is pure and becomes `sum_list (map dfail_nodes L)`. The other three are in the heap monad,
where `map` does not apply -- but `hmap`, the heap monad's own map, was already there:

```isabelle
fun hmap :: "('a ⇒ 'b Heap) ⇒ 'a list ⇒ 'b list Heap" where
  "hmap f [] = return []"
| "hmap f (x # xs) = do { y ← f x; ys ← hmap f xs; return (y # ys) }"
```

20.33 introduced it with `hmap_mono[partial_function_mono]`, which is what lets the executable
evaluator's `DAnds` clause call the fixed point through it. What was missing was the other half,
the exact analogue of `map_cong`:

```isabelle
lemma hmap_cong[fundef_cong]:
  "xs = ys ⟹ (⋀x. x ∈ set ys ⟹ f x = g x) ⟹ hmap f xs = hmap g ys"
  by (induct ys arbitrary: xs) auto
```

With both attributes `hmap` is a combinator that the function package descends through *and*
that `partial_function` accepts in a recursive position, so it serves a total definition and a
fixed point equally well. It moves up to `Monitor_Imp2`, the earliest theory that needs it, and
the three heap definitions collapse to single functions. `dformula_of` walks two lists, so its
`DAnds`, `DMatchP` and `DMatchF` clauses read `hmap (case_prod (dformula_of hp n)) (zip css L)`,
and its termination argument loses the sum type exactly as the pure evaluator's did.

No function in either session is now defined mutually with a hand-written recursion over a list.
The session builds in 5:22 (5:47 with the export) and the cram suite passes; in the generated
code `dfail_nodes_list` and `dformula_of_list` are gone and `hmap`, `zip` and `sum_list` stand
where they were. The two `Monitor_Imp2` functions were already unreachable from the export
(20.44), so the generated code does not change for them.

## 21. The five evaluators against MonPoly, and two more optimizations of the verified dense evaluator

The repository now runs five evaluators of the same fragment, and this section measures them
against each other and against MonPoly before optimizing the one that is both verified and
functional. The five, with the flags that select them:

| name | binary | flags | what runs |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pure` | `431a1da` | `-verified` | the kernel of `MFODL_Monitor_Devel`, wide tuples, pure functional structures |
| `heap_since` | `431a1da` | `-verified -heap_since` | the same kernel with the Since structure refined to Imperative HOL in `Monitor_Imp` (Section 12), verified |
| `heap_maps` | `431a1da` | `-verified -heap_maps` | the same with the unverified heap-resident maps of `Monitor_Imp2` (Section 15.3) |
| `dense` | this branch | `-verified` | the shipping monitor: `Dense_Imp.deval`, short tuples, heap structures, tested differentially, not verified |
| `vdense` | this branch | `-verified -vdense` | `Dense_Eval.deval`, the verified dense evaluator of 20.45: pure, and `dsim_deval` is about it |

`431a1da` is the commit before the dense series started; its binary is built in a worktree and
its `-verified` is the wide kernel that 20.45 measured as `monpoly_was`. The three configurations
of that binary differ only in their auxiliary structures, so the difference between them is the
refinement hypothesis of Section 2 measured once more.

### 21.1 The comparison

Wall-clock seconds, best of three interleaved runs, on the synthetic suite of 20.35 (8000 time
points, `syn.log`) and the two `P2`/`P3` formulas of Section 1 over their 201157-time-point log.
A `-` is a formula MonPoly does not accept; `refused` is a formula outside the verified dense
fragment.

| formula | monpoly | vdense | dense | heap_since | heap_maps | pure |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2 | 0.40 | 15.06 | 0.65 | 1.02 | 0.70 | 1.00 |
| P3 | 0.44 | 0.96 | 0.63 | 0.92 | 0.64 | 0.90 |
| and | 0.57 | 0.59 | 0.56 | 0.79 | 0.69 | 0.80 |
| andnot | 0.56 | 0.51 | 0.51 | 0.50 | 0.50 | 0.50 |
| once | 2.44 | 2.88 | 1.80 | 2.94 | 1.86 | 2.89 |
| since | 0.89 | 1.16 | 1.02 | 1.10 | 1.08 | 1.07 |
| eventually | 1.63 | 2.28 | 1.38 | 2.16 | 1.43 | 2.10 |
| until | 0.83 | 1.27 | 1.10 | 1.18 | 1.18 | 1.16 |
| agg | 0.61 | 0.92 | 0.88 | 0.94 | 0.94 | 0.93 |
| complex | 3.03 | 4.39 | 3.09 | 5.65 | 3.89 | 5.66 |
| sincewide | 3.18 | 2.82 | 2.73 | 3.53 | 3.46 | 3.33 |
| untilwide | 3.11 | 3.13 | 2.88 | 3.67 | 3.54 | 3.65 |
| aggwide | 12.47 | 9.81 | 9.37 | 13.14 | 11.93 | 13.12 |
| sincesub | 0.81 | 1.68 | 1.54 | 2.15 | 2.14 | 2.11 |
| untilsub | 0.69 | 1.04 | 0.97 | 1.01 | 1.00 | 1.01 |
| sincesub3 | 0.68 | 0.88 | 0.81 | 1.30 | 1.19 | 1.28 |
| trigger | - | 0.77 | 0.65 | 0.70 | 0.67 | 0.68 |
| andtrigger | - | 0.72 | 0.65 | 0.71 | 0.70 | 0.72 |
| matchp | - | refused | 0.48 | 0.47 | 0.47 | 0.47 |
| frz | - | 1.20 | 0.99 | 1.16 | 1.14 | 1.11 |
| aggonce | 0.32 | 1.22 | 0.60 | 0.64 | 0.65 | 0.65 |

Three things the table says.

**The heap refinements bought little.** `heap_since` is within noise of `pure` everywhere, and
slower on `and` and `complex`; `heap_maps` gains on `once`, `eventually` and `complex` and nothing
elsewhere. That is the conclusion of Section 15.3 restated on the current suite: the time is in
the operators and the allocation they do, not in the containers.

**The dense representation is what closed the gap with MonPoly.** `dense` is within 20% of
MonPoly on the wide formulas and ahead of it on the three widest, and its remaining deficit is
on the subformula-heavy `sincesub` and on `P2`/`P3`, where MonPoly's Since/Until keep per-tuple
timestamps in the mutable tables of Section 2.1.

**The verified dense evaluator was the shipping one, except in three places.** `vdense` and
`dense` agree within 10% on twelve of the twenty formulas. The three exceptions are `P2` (23x),
`once`/`eventually`/`P3` (1.5x) and `aggonce` (2x), and the first two have one cause each:

- `ONCE`, `EVENTUALLY` and the `NOT ONCE`/`NOT EVENTUALLY` of `P2`/`P3` have a closed left
  operand. The shipping monitor has run the sliding windows of `Since_Imp2` and `Until_Imp2`
  for those since 20.34; the verified evaluator ran the general structures, which keep a mapping
  from every tuple of the right operand to the last time point at which the (constant) left
  operand held.
- `P2` is `EVENTUALLY[0,6)` under a conjunction on a log with 106 timestamps for 201157 time
  points. The `EVENTUALLY` cannot answer for a time point until a timestamp six later arrives,
  so the conjunction above it buffers the left operand's tables for about twelve thousand time
  points, and `Dense_Eval.deval` buffers in lists with `@`. Twelve thousand appends to a list of
  twelve thousand is the 15 seconds; the shipping monitor buffers in queues.

The rest of this section removes both, keeping the theorem.

### 21.2 The sliding windows, verified (`Dense_Evt.thy`, `Dense_Evt_Aux.thy`)

`ONCE I φ` is `TT SINCE I φ` and `EVENTUALLY I φ` is `TT UNTIL I φ`; more generally the left
operand of a Since or Until is *closed* when it has no free variables (`args_L args = {}`), and
then it holds for every valuation or for none, and "the left operand held throughout" is a
property of time points alone. The general structures keep a mapping from tuples to time points
for the left operand; with a closed one the answer is the union of the right operand's tables
over a contiguous run of time points, both ends of which only move forward. A sliding window
suffices: the run's tables in a queue, the run's union as a count per tuple, and the answer as
the set of tuples whose count is positive.

**The count set.** Both windows share `cntset = (tuple, nat) mapping × table`: a multiset of
tuples as a count mapping, with the set of tuples of positive count kept alongside so that the
answer is a field rather than a scan. `cnt_add T` and `cnt_del T` fold over a table; the invariant
`cnt_inv Ts cc` says the count is `cnt_of Ts x = length (filter (λT. x ∈ T) Ts)` and the set is
`⋃ (set Ts)`, and the four lemmas `cnt_inv_snoc/tl/adds/dels` are what the window proofs use.

**Since** (`swaux = ts × queue × queue × cntset`). Two queues of `(timestamp, table)`, oldest
first: the tables whose timestamp is not yet old enough to be in the window (for a non-zero left
bound of the interval, a table *waits* rather than being excluded), and the window itself; and
the window's union as a count set. `sw_add_new_ts` moves the entries that have become old enough
from the waiting queue into the window and expires the ones that have become too old
(`sw_move`, `sw_expire`, each a scan from the front that stops at the first entry it keeps);
`sw_add_new_table` appends the new table to the window or to the waiting queue according to
`memL I 0`; `sw_join` -- the operation that in the general structure joins every stored tuple
against the left operand -- resets the whole structure when the closed left operand fails and does
nothing otherwise; `sw_result` is the count set's set. The invariant `valid_sw args cur aux
auxlist` relates the structure to the abstract `msaux` list of the kernel's locale: both queues
are sorted, the waiting entries are those not yet `memL`, the window's are those `memL ∧ memR`,
the count set is `cnt_inv` of the window's tables, and for every timestamp `u` the union of the
tables held at `u` in the two queues (`tbls_at u`) is the union of the tables at `u` in the
abstract list. The last clause is the one that makes the `join` step provable: `join_closed` says
that joining a table of width `n` over columns `R` with a closed table `rel1` (`table n {} rel1`)
is the table itself or empty according to whether `rel1` is non-empty, so "the left operand
failed" is exactly "every abstract entry becomes empty", which `tbls_at` on an empty pair of
queues also says.

**Until** (`uwaux = ts queue × entry queue × entry queue × nat × bool × cntset × table list`).
One entry `(timestamp, table, left held)` per pending time point, oldest first, split into the
entries already inside the window of the oldest pending time point and the rest; a length; a
flag saying whether the window may still grow (it stops for good at the first entry after which
the left operand fails, `bend`); the window's union as a count set; and the answers already
determined, newest first. `uw_extend` grows the window lazily, one entry at a time, as far as the
oldest time point's timestamp and the interval allow. When a new timestamp shows that the oldest
pending time point can no longer be affected by the future (`uw_shift`, a scan that stops at the
first time point still in range), `uw_step` extends its window as far as it will go, records the
window's union as that time point's answer, drops the time point's own entry from the window and
slides. `uw_add_new` shifts and appends; `uw_eval` shifts and hands over the accumulated answers.
The abstract list the kernel's `muaux` locale specifies holds, for each pending time point, the
accumulated left operand and the accumulated answer. `uw_abs args es` computes that list from the
entries: the left operand's accumulation is the unit table or empty according to whether the
left operand held at every pending time point from this one on (`uw_a1`), and the answer is the
union of the right operand's tables over the run that starts at this time point and stops at the
first one out of range or the first one after which the left operand fails (`uw_a2`). The
invariant `valid_uw` says the structure's entries are sorted and in range, the count set is
`cnt_inv` of the window, the window is a prefix of the entries closed under the extension order
(`uw_win`), and the abstract list is `ex @ uw_abs args es` where `ex` are the time points already
answered, whose answers are the reversed `dn`.

The proofs are the five (Since) and six (Until) operations against the assumptions of the
kernel's locales, specialised to a closed left operand: `valid_sw_init/add_new_ts/join/
add_new_table/result` and `valid_uw_init/length/step/mono/shift/add_new/eval`. The `join` lemma
takes `table (args_n args) {} rel1` and `(X = {}) = (rel1 = {})` as hypotheses -- the dense
evaluator's left operand table and the kernel's wide one are empty together, which is what the
simulation already knows -- and `valid_uw_shift` is proved by well-founded induction on the number
of pending entries rather than by the function package's induction, whose hypotheses do not
carry the count set through a step. 1598 lines.

**The dispatch** (`Dense_Evt_Aux.thy`) is where the verified evaluator chooses:

```isabelle
datatype dsaux_x = DSX_gen "event_data dmsaux" | DSX_win swaux
definition dsx_init args msk = (if args_L args = {} then DSX_win (sw_init args) else DSX_gen (dinit_msaux args msk))
datatype duaux_x = DUX_gen "event_data mmuaux_dq" | DUX_win uwaux
definition dux_init args = (if args_L args = {} ∧ memL (args_ivl args) 0 then DUX_win (uw_init args) else DUX_gen (init_dq args))
```

with `dsx_add_new_ts/join/add_new_table/result` and `dux_add_new/eval/length` dispatching on the
constructor, and `dsxaux'`/`duxaux'` the invariant that is the general one in the `_gen` case and
`valid_sw`/`valid_uw` in the `_win` case. `Dense_Eval`'s `DSince` and `DUntil` nodes carry
`dsaux_x`/`duaux_x`, `dsim` uses the dispatching invariants, and `dof` and `edof` initialise with
`dsx_init`/`dux_init`; the simulation theorem `dsim_deval` is unchanged in statement. The Until
window is chosen only for `memL I 0`, where the shipping structure is also chosen; the Since
window whenever the left operand is closed.

**Measured** (same suite, `vdense_was` the verified evaluator before this section):

| formula | monpoly | vdense_was | vdense (windows) | dense |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2 | 0.39 | 15.27 | 14.10 | 0.67 |
| P3 | 0.42 | 1.04 | 0.57 | 0.61 |
| once | 2.38 | 3.01 | 1.85 | 1.81 |
| eventually | 1.62 | 2.28 | 1.43 | 1.35 |
| complex | 2.96 | 4.36 | 3.27 | 3.03 |
| aggonce | 0.32 | 1.26 | 1.11 | 0.61 |

`once`, `eventually`, `P3` and `complex` are now the shipping monitor's; `P2` is untouched,
as predicted, and `aggonce` moves only a little, because its cost is elsewhere (below).

### 21.3 The buffers, in queues (`Dense_Exec.thy`)

`Dense_Eval.deval` buffers a node's operand tables in lists, as the kernel's `meval` does: a
batch is appended with `@`, a step of the traversal takes the head. That is the specification's
shape, and it is quadratic in the number of tables a buffer holds. The kernel's executable layer
(`Monitor_Impl`) solves this with `'a mbuf_t`, a copy of the list type (`typedef 'a mbuf_t =
UNIV :: 'a list set`) whose code representation is a queue (`code_datatype MBuf2_t`), an
evaluator `meeval` over a formula type `meformula` with `mbuf_t` buffers, and the theorem
`meval_code: meval j n ts db (Rep_meformula φ) = (let (zs, φ') = meeval n ts db φ in (zs,
Rep_meformula φ'))`, which is the code equation for `meval` once `code_datatype Rep_meformula`
makes the specification type's code representation the implementation type.

`Dense_Exec.thy` does the same for the dense evaluator, and nothing else. `edformula` is
`dformula` with every buffer in `mbuf_t` -- `event_data mebuf2` for the binary nodes, `mebufn`
for the multi-way conjunction, `mebuf2S` for Since, `table mbuf_t` and `ts mbuf_t` for Prev and
Next -- and `Rep_edformula :: edformula ⇒ dformula` reads the buffers through `Rep_mbuf_t`.
`edeval` is `deval` with the kernel's queue operations put in where `deval` has the list ones:
`mebuf2_add`, `mebuf2_take`, `mebuf2t_take`, `mebuf2T_take`, `mebufn_take`, `meprev_next`,
`mebuf2S_add`; the Since node's own traversal, `dsx_eval_since`, gets an `edsx_eval_since` over
`mbuf_t_cases`. The theorem is

```isabelle
lemma edeval_Rep: "deval j ts db (Rep_edformula φ) = (let (zs, φ') = edeval j ts db φ in (zs, Rep_edformula φ'))"
```

by `edeval.induct`, every case but three by the kernel's own `Rep` lemmas about the buffer
operations (`mebuf2_take`, `mebuf2t_take`, ..., as destruction rules) and the two `rep_eq`s of
`mbuf_t_empty` and `mbuf_t_append`. The three are the recursive let, which needs
`letpast_meval0_cong_f`, the freeze, which needs `default_maux.freeze_meval0_cong_fst` (the lemma
lives in the `maux` locale, so its name in the dense session is the interpretation's), and the
multi-way conjunction, whose induction hypothesis is over the zipped list. Then

```isabelle
code_datatype Rep_edformula
declare [[code drop: deval vdfail_nodes]]
lemma deval_code[code]:        "deval j ts db (Rep_edformula φ) = (let (zs, φ') = edeval j ts db φ in (zs, Rep_edformula φ'))"
lemma vdfail_nodes_code[code]: "vdfail_nodes (Rep_edformula φ) = evdfail_nodes φ"
```

and the compiler follows: `Dense_Descend_Code.edof` now builds `edformula` (`EDOr`, `EDAnd`,
... with the buffers `mbuf_t_empty @@ map (dtabc n cs) (Rep_mbuf_t b)` where `dof` writes `map
(dtabc n cs) b`), `edof_dof: Rep_edformula (edof n cs e) = dof n cs (Rep_meformula e)`, and
`dof_code[code]` the other way round. The multi-way conjunction's buffers are built from the
specification's own expression, `map (λb. mbuf_t_empty @@ b) (map2 map (map (dtabc n) css) (map
Rep_mbuf_t buf))`, because `dof` pushes `map (dtabc n)` into the column list `c0 # css @ nss` and
the simplifier then normalises the two sides of `edof_dof` differently; building the same list
and wrapping it lets both sides normalise to the same term. The generated code's `dminit_safe`,
`dmstep` and `vdfail_nodes` keep their names and their types; what they run is `edeval`.

The simulation theorem is untouched: `dsim_deval` is about `Dense_Eval.deval`, and by
`edeval_Rep` that is what the generated code computes, up to the representation of the buffers.
335 lines, plus the 166 of the retargeted compiler.

this section (20.46), `vdense_evt` with the windows only, `vdense` with the windows and the
queue buffers, `dense` the shipping monitor, all from the same commit of the front end.

| formula | monpoly | vdense_was | vdense_evt | vdense | dense |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| P2 | 0.38 | 15.16 | 13.72 | 0.61 | 0.64 |
| P3 | 0.42 | 0.96 | 0.55 | 0.56 | 0.59 |
| and | 0.57 | 0.58 | 0.57 | 0.58 | 0.55 |
| andnot | 0.56 | 0.52 | 0.51 | 0.51 | 0.50 |
| once | 2.43 | 3.02 | 1.87 | 1.85 | 1.74 |
| since | 0.89 | 1.12 | 1.16 | 1.14 | 1.02 |
| eventually | 1.64 | 2.34 | 1.42 | 1.42 | 1.37 |
| until | 0.84 | 1.25 | 1.28 | 1.26 | 1.12 |
| agg | 0.61 | 0.91 | 0.93 | 0.91 | 0.89 |
| complex | 3.00 | 4.43 | 3.28 | 3.31 | 3.13 |
| sincewide | 3.15 | 2.86 | 2.86 | 2.89 | 2.80 |
| untilwide | 3.15 | 3.16 | 3.06 | 3.12 | 2.89 |
| aggwide | 12.56 | 9.96 | 9.84 | 9.84 | 9.58 |
| sincesub | 0.82 | 1.66 | 1.69 | 1.74 | 1.50 |
| untilsub | 0.69 | 1.08 | 1.06 | 1.09 | 0.98 |

| frz | - | 1.11 | 1.04 | 1.01 | 0.96 |
| aggonce | 0.33 | 1.24 | 1.14 | 1.13 | 0.61 |

`P2` is the queue buffers: 15.2 seconds to 0.61, the shipping monitor's 0.64. The rest of the
table is the windows. Against the shipping monitor, the verified dense evaluator is now within
10% on every formula but `aggonce`; against MonPoly it keeps the shipping monitor's profile --
ahead on the wide formulas, behind by up to 2x on the subformula-heavy ones and on the two
aggregations.

**What is left, and where.** `aggonce` (`z <- CNT x ONCE[0,10] C(x)`) is the one formula where
the shipping monitor is still nearly twice as fast, and the reason is a fusion the verified
evaluator does not do (21.5 does it). The kernel folds an aggregation over a Since into the Since node
(`args_agg`), and its structure keeps the aggregate incrementally in `Optimized_Agg`'s `maggaux`
-- a count, a sum or a rank tree per group, updated as tuples enter and leave the window. The
shipping monitor keeps that fusion (20.39). The verified dense evaluator unfuses it: `dof` puts a
`DAgg` node over a `DSince` whose window reports the plain union, and `dagg` recomputes the count
per group from the whole union at every time point (the `DsSinceAgg` case of `dsim`). Since the
window already maintains the union as a count set with the tuples' entries and exits explicit
(`cnt_add`, `cnt_del`), attaching `maggaux` to those transitions is what 21.5 does, and
`valid_insert_maggaux`/`valid_delete_maggaux`/`valid_result_maggaux` are its proof.

The remaining 5-10% on `since`, `until`, `sincesub` and `trigger` is the representation of a
tuple: the verified evaluator's tables hold `event_data option list`s with no `None` in them
(20.45), the shipping monitor's the unboxed `event_data list` of 20.36. That is not an
algorithmic difference and not one that the simulation theorem sees; it is a data refinement of
the table type, and it would be done the way `mbuf_t` is done here -- a copy of the type with a
different code representation -- if the kernel's table operations were not themselves stated on
the boxed type.

**The differential tests** are unchanged: on the twenty-one benchmark formulas `-verified
-vdense` and `-verified` give identical verdicts (and MonPoly too where it accepts the formula),
with `matchp` refused by the verified fragment as before; of the 600 single-line `-verified`
commands of the cram corpus and the `cov_verified` formulas, 593 are identical, 7 refused, 0
differ. `MFODL_Monitor_Imp` builds in 4:32 elapsed on four threads, export included.

### 21.5 The aggregation over a ONCE, fused into the window (`Dense_Evt_Agg.thy`)

21.4 left one formula where the shipping monitor was nearly twice as fast: `aggonce`,
`z <- CNT x ONCE[0,10] C(x)`. The kernel folds the aggregation into the Since node and keeps the
aggregate incrementally inside its structure; the verified dense evaluator compiled the same node
to a `DAgg` over a `DSince` whose window reports the plain union, and recomputed the count per
group from that union at every time point. This section fuses the two for the verified
evaluator, keeping the theorem.

**What the kernel already has.** `Optimized_Agg.thy` is a verified incremental aggregation
structure: `aggaux` is a count, a sum or a rank tree per group, `insert_maggaux`/`delete_maggaux`
take a set of tuples in or out (each returning a flag that says whether the structure could keep
the aggregate, so that the caller can fall back), and

```isabelle
valid_insert_maggaux: valid_maggaux args aux X ⟹ finite Y ⟹ X ∩ Y = {} ⟹ let (v', aux') = insert_maggaux args Y aux in if v' then valid_maggaux args aux' (X ∪ Y) else True
valid_delete_maggaux: valid_maggaux args aux X ⟹ Y ⊆ X ⟹ let (v', aux') = delete_maggaux args Y aux in if v' then valid_maggaux args aux' (X - Y) else True
valid_result_maggaux: valid_maggaux args aux X ⟹ result_maggaux args aux = eval_aggargs args X
```

are its contract. The kernel's Since structure with an aggregation carries an `aggaux option`
next to its tables and drives it with the tuples that enter and leave (`Optimized_Agg_Temporal`).
The window of 21.2 has exactly those events: its count set makes a tuple's entry into the union
(`cnt_enter T cc = T - snd cc`, the tuples of a new table not yet in the union) and its exit
(`cnt_leave T cc`, the tuples of an expiring table that the union holds exactly once) explicit.
So the fused window is the window plus an `aggaux option`, with `ag_ins`/`ag_del` called at every
`cnt_add`/`cnt_del`, and the invariant `ag_inv` saying the structure is valid for the union.

**The one real obstacle: the frame.** `valid_maggaux` is stated for the kernel's aggregation
`eval_agg n g0 y ω tys f`, which reads and writes tuples that have a slot for the result column
`y`. The dense aggregation `dagg g0 ω f k pos` (Section 20) reads tuples *without* that slot and
inserts the result at position `pos`. The two are reconciled by `dagg_p_eval_aggargs`:

```isabelle
dagg_ok p w ⟹ (∀d ∈ D. dwf_tuple w d) ⟹
  dagg_p p D = dtabc n' [0..<n'] (eval_aggargs (dagg_args p w) (dagg_wide p w D))
```

-- the dense aggregation of a table of width `w` is the kernel's aggregation, at a frame `n' = w
- k + 1` whose group columns are all of the result's columns but `pos`, of the table widened by
one `None` at position `k + pos` (`dagg_wide`, an instance of `dtabw`), with the term's variables
above the slot shifted by one (`shift_trm`), and the result narrowed back. It is an instance of
`dagg_eval_agg` (20.2) at that small frame, and it is what lets the fused window feed the kernel's
structure `dagg_wide p w (cnt_enter T cc)` and report `dtabc n' [0..<n'] (result_maggaux …)`. The
widening is injective on well-formed tuples (`inj_on_dwiden`), which is what turns the count set's
set-level facts about entering and leaving tuples into the disjointness and subset side
conditions of the kernel's lemmas.

**The dispatch and the simulation.** `dsaux_x` gets a third constructor, `DSX_wagg p a`, and two
projections that say what a structure reports relative to the union: `dsx_agg` (the parameters of
the fused aggregation, if any) and `dsx_post` (`dagg_p p` or `id`), both preserved by every
operation and by a firing of the node (`dsx_agg_eval_since`). The simulation's result relation
`dsres` becomes `dsresh n cs args h`, with the post-function `h` applied to the dense side, and
`eval_since_rel` is proved for it with `h = dsx_post daux` carried through the traversal. Then
`dsim` gets one new rule, `DsSinceFused`: a `DSince` whose structure reports the aggregate
simulates the kernel's fused `MSince` directly, with the same twenty-two side conditions as
`DsSinceAgg` (the unfused pair) and `dsx_agg daux = Some (…)` in place of `dsx_agg daux = None`;
its case in `dsim_deval_N` is the `DsSinceAgg` case with the `dagg_eval_agg` step moved from
the `DAgg` node into the structure. `dof` and `edof` choose the fused window when the left
operand has no join columns (`cpos cs_R (set cs_L) = {}`) -- the condition is stated on the
column positions rather than on `cs_L = []` because an equation on `cs_L` is a rewrite rule the
simplifier would use inside the branch, and `edof_dof`'s induction hypothesis is stated on
`cs_L`. `dsim_dof_minit0_MSinceAgg` gets the corresponding case, with `dsince_rel_init_agg`
supplying the initial state.

One thing to record from the executable layer. After the datatype grew its constructor, the
buffer lemma `edsx_eval_since` of `Dense_Exec` (unchanged in statement) failed inside `auto`
with an internal `TYPE` exception, "Variable has two distinct types ?x1", whenever the first
induction hypothesis was chained through the simplifier -- with the same hypothesis proved fine
by explicit resolution. The hypothesis's binders are consistently typed (an ML check confirmed
it), so the clash arises in the simplifier's conversion of the premise to a rewrite rule and I
did not find its root; the proof now applies that hypothesis with `OF` in the one branch that
needs it and leaves the rest to `auto`.

Sizes: `Dense_Evt_Agg.thy` is 675 lines (the frame lemma with its column-list arithmetic, the
aggregate alongside the count set, the two scans re-stated with the aggregate threaded through
and proved against the plain ones, and the five validity lemmas); the dispatch grows by 130, the
simulation by 210 and the two compilers by 80.

**Measured** (same suite; `dense` the shipping monitor):

| formula | monpoly | vdense (21.4) | vdense (fused) | dense |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| aggonce | 0.31 | 1.13 | 0.57 | 0.61 |
| agg | 0.59 | 0.91 | 0.91 | 0.87 |
| aggwide | 12.33 | 9.84 | 9.93 | 9.29 |
| once | 2.37 | 1.85 | 1.85 | 1.74 |
| since | 0.87 | 1.14 | 1.13 | 1.02 |
| complex | 2.95 | 3.31 | 3.26 | 3.02 |

`aggonce` is now the shipping monitor's; nothing else moved, as nothing else compiles to the
fused window (the `agg` formulas aggregate a non-temporal operand and go through `DAgg` as
before). The remaining gap to MonPoly on the two aggregations is MonPoly's own, unverified,
mutable aggregation tables; the remaining 5-15% to the shipping monitor on `since`, `until`,
`sincesub` and `trigger` is the boxed tuple representation of 21.4.

The differential tests are as before: identical verdicts on the benchmark formulas (with
`matchp` refused), and 593 identical, 7 refused, 0 differing on the corpus.
`MFODL_Monitor_Imp` builds in 4:34 on four threads with the export.

**Not done: the same for an EVENTUALLY.** The Until window of 21.2 has the same structure -- a
count set with explicit entries and exits -- so an aggregation over an `EVENTUALLY` could be
fused the same way, with the aggregate read at the moment a time point's window is complete
(`uw_step`) rather than at every result. Nothing in the suite exercises it, and the kernel's
own Until structure does not fuse its aggregation either, so it was left as it is.

**Where the verified dense evaluator stands.** Against the pure kernel it started from
(`vdense_was` in 21.2's table, itself the result of Sections 19-20), the three changes of this
section are 1.7x on `once`, 1.6x on `eventually` and `P3`, 2.2x on `aggonce`, and 25x on `P2`;
it is within 10% of the shipping, unverified monitor on every formula of the suite it accepts,
ahead of MonPoly on the wide formulas, and behind MonPoly by up to 2x on the subformula-heavy
ones. Every one of those changes is covered by the simulation theorem `dsim_deval`, whose
statement did not change.

### 21.6 The verified dense evaluator on short tuples (`Dense_Unbox_Eval.thy`, `Dense_Unbox_Exec.thy`)

Section 21.4 left the verified dense evaluator on the kernel's boxed tuples, `event_data option
list`, while the shipping monitor ran on `event_data list`, and put the remaining 5-15% gap on
`since`, `until`, `sincesub` and `trigger` down to that.  This section removes the box from the
verified evaluator: `-verified -vdense` now runs `udeval`, an evaluator over `event_data list`
tables whose every node, buffer and temporal structure holds short tuples, and it is covered by
the simulation theorem of `Dense_Eval.thy` through one refinement theorem.

**The design: exact naturality, no invariants.** The shipping monitor's unboxing (20.31-20.37)
argued from the hole-free discipline, `dwf_tuple`, that no dense operation constructs a `None`.
Carrying such an invariant through a *verified* evaluator would have meant threading it through
every case of `dsim_deval`.  The route taken instead is a refinement without any invariant: a
map `reopt = map Some` from short tuples to boxed ones, lifted to tables (`reopt_table`), to the
queues, deques, buffers and mappings the structures are built from, and to the formula
(`Rep_udformula`), such that every operation of the boxed evaluator commutes with the map on
*every* input -- not only on hole-free ones.  The theorem is then an equation,

    edeval j ts (rdb db) (Rep_udformula φ) = (case udeval j ts db φ of (zs, φ') ⇒ (map reopt_table zs, Rep_udformula φ'))

(`udeval_Rep`), with `rdb` the embedding of the database, and it composes with `edeval_Rep` and
`dsim_deval` as is.  The step theorem `udmstep_Rep` says the same of the monitor step.

Making every operation exactly natural took three preparatory changes to the verified
evaluator, each a refactoring under the same simulation theorem (`Dense_Utuple.thy`,
`Dense_Until_Gen.thy`; commit "state the field readers, the walks and the Until deque so that
short tuples refine them exactly"):

- *The field readers.* The operations that read a field -- the match, the constraint, the
  assignment, the aggregation, the time-point and time-stamp terms -- are not natural: `the
  (v ! x)` on a hole differs from anything a short tuple can produce.  They are now *defined*
  on boxed tuples as the short-tuple twin read through `deopt = map the` and written back
  through `reopt` (`dmatch_table tms cs X = reopt_table (umatch_table tms cs (deopt_table X))`,
  and likewise `dagg`, `dassign1`, `deval_constraint`).  On hole-free tuples that is what they
  were (`deopt_reopt`), so the simulation proofs did not change; on every input it is natural
  by construction.
- *The walks.* Every fold over a table goes through `csl X = (if finite X then
  csorted_list_of_set X else [])`, whose commutation with `reopt_table` is a fact about the
  comparators (`csl_reopt_table`: the list comparator on `Some`s is the list comparator).
- *The Until deque.* The kernel's deque reads entries by counter-derived indices, which are
  only meaningful under the deque invariant; a map over the entries of an inconsistent deque
  need not commute with such a read.  The generic Until structure of `Dense_Until_Gen.thy`
  reads its front entry and its indexed entries off the lists (`dq_hd_s`, `dq_nth_l`), and its
  nested update (`gupd_nested_step_dq`) does the same; the agreement with the kernel's reads is
  proved once, under the invariant the kernel maintains (`duaux'_ushape`), so that the
  evaluator's proofs see the kernel's operations and the refinement sees natural ones.  The one
  operation that cannot be made natural is the evaluation step's read of the time-stamp queue's
  head, undefined on an empty queue: it is natural on every state it is applied to, and the
  shift applies it once per time stamp it takes (`foldl_geval_step_dq_rmuaux`, with the length
  of the queue as the side condition).
- *The sanitiser.* Where a table enters from a kernel structure the evaluator does not
  otherwise inspect -- the trigger's result, the fused aggregation's result, a `DRel` -- it
  passes through `dsan = reopt_table ∘ deopt_table`, the identity on hole-free tables, so that
  the unboxed evaluator reads the structure's result as `deopt_table` and the boxed one as its
  embedding, exactly.  The trigger itself keeps its boxed buffers and structure: its left
  operand is embedded into the columns of the right one *with holes*, which no short tuple has.

`Dense_Unbox_Eval.thy` (1900 lines) states the naturality of every container -- `map_mbuf`,
`map_queue`, `map_dq`, and `rmk`, which re-keys a mapping from short to boxed tuples through
`those` -- and of every structure: the generic Since (`rmsaux`), the Since window (`gsw_*`,
generic copies of `sw_*` with the generic count set), the fused aggregation window (`usa_*`,
with `uag_ins`/`uag_del`/`uag_res` widening the short tuples into the kernel's aggregation
structure in one pass, `udwiden`), the Until deque (`rmuaux`) and the Until window (`uuw_*`).
`Dense_Unbox_Exec.thy` (1240 lines) has the dispatched structures, the buffers, the join, the
formula `udformula`, `udeval`, the theorem, and the compiler `udof` with `udof_edof`, the state
`udmstate` and the step `udmstep`.  The compiler is stated on the specification's formula through
`Abs_meformula`, the inverse of the kernel's `Rep_meformula`, so that `udminit_safe` applies to
`minit_safe`'s state and runs on the executable one.

Things to record from the proofs:

- The simplifier of this development unfolds `Set.filter` into a comprehension, so naturality
  rules on filters must be stated in the shape the simplifier produces
  (`Collect_lookup_rmk`, `filter_dproj_reopt_table`), and the default weak congruence rule for
  `case` leaves branches unsimplified unless the case is split (`gupd_nested_step_dq_eq`).
- A rule stated with `id` never matches, because the goal has `λa. a`; every map is written
  `(λv. v)`.  Rules stated on an abstract pair (`rcc cc`) must have the pair's `simps` deleted,
  or the simplifier splits the pair first and the rule never applies.
- The recursive functions' `simps` loop as rewrite rules (`meprev_next`, `edsx_eval_since`,
  the queue scans); the naturality proofs of the recursive structures use `subst` on both
  sides and then the induction hypothesis, and the evaluator's proof deletes them.
- A function-package induction over `letpast_meval0 eval …` generalises the evaluator
  argument; an assumption about the evaluator stated outside the induction is then about a
  different variable of the same name.  The assumption goes into the statement.
- The bulk `auto` over the eighteen structural cases of `udeval_Rep` did not terminate; the
  cases are proved one by one with the naturality rules each needs, in the shapes the
  simplifier leaves the buffers in (`meprev_next_prev`, `meprev_next_next1-3`,
  `edsx_eval_since_rdsx0`, `mebuf2T_take_acc0`).

**The code.** `Monitor_Code_Imp.thy` exports `udminit_safe`, `udmstep`, `uvdfail_nodes` and
`udmstate_m` in place of the boxed `dminit_safe`/`dmstep`, and the wide database and its
functions are no longer exported; `verified_adapter.ml` runs `-vdense` on the short database and
prints short verdicts, and the wide-tuple conversion, comparison and printing are gone (98
lines).  The generic window and count set operations, `gupd_nested_step_dq`, `udtabw`,
`deopt_table` and `reopt_table` join the monomorphisation list of `Dense_Code_Opt.thy`
(which now imports `Dense_Unbox_Exec`: a constant that is not in scope there is read as a free
variable and the monomorphiser reports "Illegal fixed variable").

**Measured.** The suite of `evaluation/perf` (185 formulas; `report.md` and `ratios.svg` there are this
run), against the run of 21.5 on the same machine, where MonPoly's own times moved by 1%:
