`mbuf2_take_rel2` and `dandbuf_add`/`dandbuf_take`, in the same style as the other binary nodes.

### The executable mirrors

`EDAndTrigger` in `Dense_Exec.thy` and `UDAndTrigger` in `Dense_Unbox_Exec.thy` are the same
node over queue buffers and then over short tuples, with `edeval_Rep`, `edof_dof`, `udeval_Rep`
and `udof_edof` extended to them.  The unboxed node reads the trigger structure without `dsan`,
because it already works on short tuples; the relabelling lemmas
`mebuf2T_take_andtrigger` and `mebuf2_take_andtrigger` carry the two stages across `reopt`.

### Tests

The cram test `verimon_tests/vdense_andtrigger.t` runs eleven such formulas through `-verified`
and `-verified -vdense` and compares their output.  They cover a trigger at the conjunct's
frame, a narrower one, a bounded and an unbounded window, a positive lower bound, the node
under a quantifier, a conjunction and a temporal operator, and the nullary case.  All agree,
and the `verimon_tests` and `tests` suites pass.  The corpus now runs entirely under `-vdense`:
600 same, 0 refused, 0 differing (593/7/0 before).

### The suite

Five formulas, one new group, all on the base log: a short, a longer and an unbounded window,
a positive lower bound, and a trigger narrower than its conjunct.  MonPoly does not monitor
TRIGGER, so the group has no ratio against it; it is there for the two verified monitors.

| | vdense s | dense s | vdense / dense |
|---|---|---|---|
| `atrig-5` | 0.81 | 0.85 | 0.95 |
| `atrig-50` | 0.81 | 1.04 | 0.78 |
| `atrig-lb` | 1.44 | 1.62 | 0.88 |
| `atrig-inf` | 1.01 | 0.98 | 1.03 |
| `atrig-narrow` | 0.84 | 0.98 | 0.85 |
| geomean | | | **0.89** |

The verified dense evaluator is level with the shipping dense monitor on this group, if
anything slightly ahead.  That is expected: the shipping node in `Dense_Imp.thy` runs the same
two stages over the same narrowed trigger structure (`dargs cs_R cs_L`), and differs only in
wrapping the conjunct's tables in an option and joining through its heap index.  Against the
shipping monitor's times from the pre-change run on the same container (below), the ratios are
0.82 to 0.98, so the 0.89 is within this container's noise and should not be read as an advantage.

This run was on a different container from the earlier sections, a slower and noisier one.
MonPoly takes 1.22x its time of the 21.24 run there, and the whole-suite ratio vdense/monpoly
reads 1.35 against 1.29.  To separate the machine from the change, the pre-change binary
(21.24) ran the whole suite on the same container right after: the new binary's times are
0.93x the old one's for MonPoly, 0.89x for `-vdense` and 0.91x for the shipping monitor.  The
change adds a node and touches nothing else in the generated code, so all three moving
together is drift between the two runs, not a regression.  The two `-vdense` timeouts of this
run, `long-sum100` and `pol-unmatched`, time out under the old binary on this container too;
at 21.24 on the other machine they took under 5 s.

## 21.26 `-verified` runs the verified dense evaluator

Until now the binary had two dense monitors.  `-verified` ran the heap-resident one of
`Dense_Imp.thy` (session `MFODL_Monitor_Imp`): an Imperative HOL evaluator with a hash-join index
on the heap, compiled from the kernel's formula.  It uses the proved short-tuple operations, and
its multi-way join is proved correct (`dmulti_join_rule`, `Dense_Join_Correct.thy`), but its
compiler and its evaluation function are not proved to simulate the kernel.  `-verified -vdense`
ran the verified dense evaluator (`udminit_safe`/`udmstep`), which the simulation theorem of
`Dense_Eval.thy` covers through the refinements of `Dense_Exec.thy` and `Dense_Unbox_Exec.thy`,
and which refused any formula with a node its compiler could not place.  21.25 removed the last
such node: on the corpus `-vdense` refuses nothing.  So `-verified` now runs the verified
evaluator, and the heap-resident monitor is out of the binary.

### The export

`src/verified.ml` is now the export of `MFODL_Monitor_Dense.Monitor_Code_Dense`, not of
`MFODL_Monitor_Imp.Monitor_Code_Imp`.  That export already existed for reading (it was checked
to reach nothing of the Imp session), but it was not the one the binary was built from, because
the code-generator tuning of 20.x and 21.x -- the monomorphised containers, the tree primitives
with the comparator called directly, the dictionaries built once (`Dense_Code_Opt.thy`) and the
sweep over the remaining generic operations (`Dense_Mono_Sweep.thy`) -- lived in the Imp session.
Both theories move into `MFODL_Monitor_Dense`.  Only two parts of them are about the heap: the
box of the heap index of the dense join (`im_lookup_h` and its neighbours) and the two
specialisations of the heap-resident Since (`hsd_*`, `hsw_*`).  They stay in the Imp session as
`Dense_Imp_Code_Opt.thy`.  `Monitor_Code_Imp` now exports only the heap-resident monitor, to a
file of its own, `verified_imp.ocaml`, that is not linked into the binary.
`Monitor_Code_Imp_Only`, which did that alongside the combined export, goes.

Moving the theories changed how the code preprocessor's rules are merged, and with it the
generated code, in two ways.  Both are invisible to correctness, since every rule involved is a
proved code equation for the same function, but both undo part of E2's monomorphisation:

- Where a box of `Dense_Code_Opt` (`card` ↦ `unbox edl_card_v`) and a monomorphic copy from
  `Dense_Mono_Sweep`'s second pass (`card` ↦ `edl_card`) rewrite the same term, the preprocessor
  takes whichever rule the merge of the theory branches puts first.  With `Dense_Mono_Sweep`
  imported before `Monitor_Code` the box won, at `card`, `Set.remove`, `cluster`,
  `rbt_of_sorted_list`, `deopt_table` and the deletion of a key set.  Importing `Monitor_Code`
  first restores the old choice.  (`code_thms` on a one-line definition in a scratch theory
  showed which rule wins for each order.)
- A merge takes the union of the two preprocessor simpsets, so it brings back rules that one
  branch deleted.  `Dense_Code_Opt` takes the redirections of the wide set operations to their
  boxes (`ed_union_v_unfold` and four more) out of the preprocessor so that the monomorphic
  `ed_union` etc. apply; `Monitor_Code` brings them back.  `Monitor_Code_Dense` now deletes
  them again after the merge.

With both, every function the new export shares with the old one is textually identical to it,
up to whitespace and the name of the sum monad's bind (`binda` before, `bind` now that the heap
monad's bind is gone).  What the new export lacks is the heap-resident monitor's own code, 196
items (`deval_step`, `dformula_of`, `minit_safe_d`, the heap index, the `hsd_*` Since, the typerep
and heap class instances); it has none the old one lacked.  `src/verified.ml` shrinks from 14,512
lines to 11,377.

### The front end

`Verified_adapter` has one state type and one step.  A formula with a node outside the verified
dense fragment is refused with the same message `-vdense` used, which no formula of the corpus
triggers.  `-vdense` is accepted and ignored, like the other deprecated flags.  The two cram
tests that compared `-verified` with `-verified -vdense` (21.24, 21.25) become plain `-verified`
tests: their expected output was recorded from the heap-resident monitor, so they now check the
switch itself.  They are renamed `dense_match.t` and `dense_andtrigger.t`.  The performance
runner drops the `dense` system; `vdense` keeps its name in the reports but is now plain
`-verified`.

### Checks

Both sessions build.  `dune test` passes.  The new `-verified` agrees with the old one (the 21.25
binary, whose `-verified` was the heap-resident monitor) on the whole corpus: 600 same, 0
refused, 0 differing.  It also agrees on the 21 benchmark formulas of section 1 and the later
sections, and with MonPoly on the 17 of them MonPoly monitors.

### What the switch costs

The verified evaluator's own code is unchanged, so the question is only how it compares with the
monitor `-verified` used to run.  The 21.25 run measured both in one suite on one container:

| over the 192 formulas both finished | vdense / dense |
|---|---|
| geomean | **0.975** |
| median | 0.99 |
| vdense more than 10% faster | 44 |
| vdense more than 10% slower | 24 |
| vdense more than 25% slower | 10 |

On most of the suite the switch is free, or a little better.  The exception is the temporal regex
nodes: nine of the ten formulas that lose more than 25% are `match-*`, at 1.29x to 2.48x
(`match-pstar`).  21.24 said why: the verified match node runs the kernel's window of tabulated
derivatives over boxed tuples and converts at its boundary, where the heap-resident monitor had
a window at its own frame.  The tenth is `andnot-dom50` (1.37x), a single anti-join on the
small-domain log.  A dense match window over short tuples, in the shape of the Since and Until
structures of sections 13 and 16, is what would close the regex gap; it is the obvious first
item for H.

The suite after the switch, on yet another container (MonPoly at 0.81x its time of the 21.25
run, so again not comparable with earlier runs): all 201 formulas finish under `-verified`, with
no timeouts; over the 196 that MonPoly monitors, the geometric mean of `-verified`/MonPoly is
1.25, median 1.22.  32 formulas are faster under `-verified`, 9 more than 2x slower, and none
more than 4x slower.  The worst family is `once_exists` (1.78), then `agg_once` (1.56), `match`
(1.53) and `policy` (1.52).

## 21.27 H: the three worst families after the switch

The 21.26 run ranked the families by the geometric mean of `-verified`/MonPoly: `once_exists`
(1.78), `agg_once` (1.56), `match` (1.53) and `policy` (1.52).  `match` has had its dense nodes
since 21.24; what it still pays for is the kernel's derivative window, whose replacement 21.26
already priced.  H took the worst formula of the other kinds: the existential over a window
(`onceex-50`), the extremum over a window (`aggonce-max50`), and the Since with a negated
left operand behind most of `policy` (`since-nu50`).  The first is fixed, the second improved,
the third priced and declined.

### H1: the existential moves into the window

Over a window whose left operand is `TT` -- `ONCE` and `EVENTUALLY` -- an existential commutes
with the window: `∃y. ONCE[I] φ` holds exactly when `ONCE[I] (∃y. φ)` does.  The two monitor
very differently.  With the quantifier outside, the window keeps every tuple of `φ` over the
whole interval and the quantifier projects that whole result at every time point; with it
inside, the projection runs once, on the entering tuples, and the window keeps only the
projected ones.  On `onceex-50` (`C(x) AND (EXISTS y. ONCE[0,50] A(x,y))`) the window holds
about 2000 pairs against 200 values of `x`, and the projection was 71% of the run.

`push_ex` (`Dense_Push.thy`) makes the move everywhere it applies, and nowhere inside `LET`,
`LETPAST`, the freeze quantifier, the regular expressions, the trigger and release operators,
or a conjunction with a trigger or release on the right: those are the formulas whose
treatment builds new formulas from their operands, and leaving them alone keeps the lemmas
structural.  The lemmas show that `push_ex` preserves everything the monitor's specification
depends on -- free variables, satisfaction, progress, the relevant events, safety in both
directions, future-boundedness and typing -- so `verimon_spec.dmonitor_mverdicts_push` states
that the dense monitor started on `push_ex φ` computes exactly `Mt` for `φ`.  The front end
calls `udminit_push = udminit_safe ∘ push_ex`.  Formula equality is not executable (the
intervals have no equality in the generated code), so the test for `TT` gets a code equation
that reads its shape.

| formula | before | after | MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|
| `onceex-50` | 3.77 s | 0.63 s | 1.35 s |
| `onceex-not50` | 3.49 s | 0.58 s | 1.77 s |
| `onceex-20` | 1.92 s | 0.60 s | 1.00 s |

The output is identical on all three; the cram test `dense_push.t` runs 18 formulas, including
the shapes the rewrite leaves alone.

### H2: the extremum over a window

`aggonce-max50` (`r <- MAX y; x ONCE[0,50] A(x,y)`) keeps, per group, an order-statistic tree
of the window's values (`Order_Statistic_Tree.thy`, a weight-balanced tree with sizes in the
nodes, under `Tree_List.thy`'s `treelist` and `Optimized_Agg.thy`'s `list_aux`).  The profile
charged it in three places, and `Dense_Agg_Code.thy` refines all three with code equations
proved against the ones they replace:

* **Reading the extremum.**  MIN and MAX asked for the element of rank 0 and of rank `n - 1`,
  and `select` finds it by comparing the rank with the stored size at every node on the way
  down -- natural-number arithmetic that the generated code does through Zarith.  That read was
  201 M instructions of max50's 3.35 G.  `wbt_min` and `wbt_max` walk to the leftmost and the
  rightmost node instead; `select t 0 = wbt_min t` and `select t (size_wbt t - 1) = wbt_max t`
  on every tree satisfying `wbt`, which every tree inside `wf_wbt` does, and a `code_unfold`
  rule redirects the two reads of the result.
* **Deleting.**  `combine`, which joins the two subtrees of a deleted node, compared `size`
  of both, which walks them, although every node carries its size; and `split_min`/`del_max`
  tested `l = Leaf`, an equality on trees, which was the only reason the tree operations took an
  `equal` dictionary.  `delete_p` reads the stored size and matches on `Leaf`; it equals `delete`
  under `wbt`, and is `tree_remove`'s code equation.
* **The dictionaries.**  21.16 specialised the treelist operations at `integer` and `string8`, but
  their bodies still called the generic tree operations, whose comparison `cmp` went through the
  dictionary's `less` and `equal` at every node.  The tree operations now have their copies at
  both types too, and `select` its `nat` comparison.  The exported code has no dictionary left on
  the rank path.

The subtrees are inspected with a `case` rather than by nested patterns: `fun` compiles a
nested pattern `Node (Node l' x r') y r` into code that rebuilds the inner node before passing
it on, an allocation per level.

The result table was measured too, and left alone.  `ugmr` builds it by inserting its rows one
at a time into a persistent red-black tree; the alternative -- collect the rows, sort them with
the comparator merge sort `sort_c`, build the tree with `rbt_of_sorted_list` -- was proved (the
equation and its proof were three lines over `ugmr_code`) and measured.  Section 15.7 found that
construction wrong for the probe join, whose list can be far longer than its table; here there is
one row per group, so that case does not arise, and it still does not pay: on `aggonce-cnt50` it
saved 190 M instructions of insertion and rebalancing and spent 170 M on the merge sort, the
sortedness check of `rbt_of_sorted_list` and the natural-number arithmetic of `rbtreeify`, net
0.7% of the run.  The experiment that had suggested it used OCaml's `List.sort_uniq`, which the
verified code does not have.

Measured, instructions exactly on the 1500-point `base` log of the profiles, and wall time on the
suite's `base` log (median of seven runs, interleaved, on one container):

| formula | instructions, before → after | wall, before → after | MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|
| `aggonce-max50` | 3.347 G → 3.091 G (−7.6%) | 2.14 s → 2.02 s | 0.91 s |
| `aggonce-min50` | 3.228 G → 3.072 G (−4.8%) | 2.12 s → 2.00 s | 0.96 s |
| `aggonce-med20` | 4.455 G → 4.401 G (−1.2%) | 2.49 s → 2.36 s | 1.66 s |
| `aggonce-cnt50` (code unchanged) | 2.704 G → 2.704 G (0.0%) | 1.61 s → 1.68 s | 1.08 s |

MAX gains more than MIN because `select`'s descent to the last rank subtracts at every node, where
the descent to rank 0 only compares; MED still selects by rank and gains only the deletions and
the comparisons.  The controls' wall times (`aggonce-cnt50` +4%, `since-nu50` +3%) are this container's
noise, which is why the instruction counts are the measurement.  H2 is therefore a modest
improvement, and MIN and MAX stay at about 2.1-2.2x MonPoly.  What is left of them is no longer the
rank tree -- its insertions and deletions are now 5.7% of `aggonce-max50` -- but what every
aggregation over a window pays: the result table (20.5%), and the per-group mapping's upkeep as
tuples enter (19.8%) and leave (19.2%) the window, which 21.22 priced.  The cram test
`dense_rank.t` runs MIN, MAX and MED over sliding windows on a log with repeated values, grouped
and ungrouped, on integers and strings, with the output recorded from the verified monitor
before the change (and equal to MonPoly's).

### H3: Since with a negated left operand, declined

`since-nu50` (`B(x,y) AND ((NOT C(x)) SINCE[0,50] A(x,y))`) runs at about 2x MonPoly.  Two thirds
of the run is the Since node, and all of it is red-black-tree work on the node's five
structures: the window's table, its index by the left operand's columns, the per-tuple
timestamps, and the same two for the tuples since the last match.  Adding a time point's tuples
is 29% of the run, the negated operand's garbage collection and join 34% (of which the join is
24%); red-black insertion, deletion and rebalancing make up the bulk of both.  The join is
already key-driven -- for a negated operand it deletes the operand's keys from the index rather
than filtering the index (21.8) -- and the one remaining generic piece, the collection's
union of the window's tuple sets, was rewritten as a fold of `edl_union` in an experiment and
bought 1.9%.

What is left is the price of five persistent trees against MonPoly's hash tables, and a hash
table is imperative: the verified structures of this session are pure functions, and the
heap-resident refinement of session MFODL_Monitor_Imp, which is where an imperative structure
would go, is not what `-verified` runs (21.26).  Halving the node's work -- which no change to
the pure structures in sight would do -- would take `since-nu50` from 2.0x to about 1.3x.

## 21.28 The suite after 21.27, and the front end's predicate filter

The run after 21.27 (`evaluation/perf/report.md`, 8f9de56) puts `-verified` at a geometric mean
of 1.33x MonPoly over the 193 formulas both finish, with three `-verified` timeouts and 13
formulas more than 2x slower.

**The timeouts are not blow-ups.**  That container runs MonPoly 1.31x as slowly as the one of
this section (geometric mean over 194 formulas; 2.2x on the \`long\` log), and with two jobs at
once; on this one, without a timeout, the three formulas
take 2.87 s (`aggonce-rate80`, 1.8x MonPoly), 1.84 s (`long-ev1000`, 1.4x) and 2.90 s
(`long-sum100`, 1.0x -- MonPoly times out on it there too).

**The 30 largest slowdowns fall into four groups:**

| group | of the 30 | examples | what it pays for |
|---|---|---|---|
| grouped aggregation over a past window | 14 | `aggonce-*`, `pol-threshold`, `pol-sumfilter`, `aggguard-sum` | the result table and the per-group mapping, rebuilt or updated at every time point |
| Since/Until with a negated unary left operand | 7 | `since-nu*`, `pol-sincefut(b)`, `until-nu20` | the node's five persistent trees (21.27, declined) |
| MATCHP/MATCHF | 6 | `match-pseq`, `match-pstar` | the kernel's derivative window (21.24, 21.26) |
| a fixed cost of the front end | 2 | `prev-unary`, `join-self3` | every event converted, see below |

The suite's ratios are larger than this container's (`aggonce-cnt5` is 2.21x there and 1.50x here;
its instruction ratio is 1.21x).  `-verified` allocates two to five times what MonPoly does
(`aggonce-cnt5`: 544 M words of minor heap against 116 M), and two jobs sharing a container's
memory bandwidth cost the one that allocates more.  One lead was ruled out on the way:
`-verified` compacts its heap far more often (83 compactions on `aggonce-cnt5` against 2), because
its `space_overhead` of 500 meets the default `max_overhead` of 500; switching compaction off
changed no wall time measurably.

### The predicate filter

On `prev-unary` (`PREV[0,1] C(x)`) the lexer costs the same in both systems, and `-verified`'s
extra 181 M instructions are in the unverified front end: every event of every predicate was
converted to short tuples, sorted and built into a table at every time point, although the
formula reads only `C`, 10 of the 100 events per time point.  `main.ml` enabled `Filter_rel` for
both systems, and MonPoly's `Algorithm.tuple` consults it, but `Algorithm_verified.tuple` did not.
It now drops the events of predicates the formula does not mention (`Filter_rel.rel_OK`, which
treats the names that `LET`, `LETPAST` and the freeze quantifier bind as bound); `-nofilterrel`
switches it off for both systems.  Only whole predicates are dropped, not tuples by their
constants, and the time points stay: `end_tp` runs for every one.  That this preserves the output
is `sat_relevant_events'` (`Sat.thy`): satisfaction depends on the events of the formula's own
predicates only.

The corpus is unchanged (600 same, 0 differing), and the cram test `verified_filter.t` covers
time points with only filtered events (PREV, NEXT, the windows, an aggregation, MATCHP) and events
of a predicate that `LET` binds.  Ratio to MonPoly, median of five, on this section's container:

| formula | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `prev-unary` | 1.41 | 0.97 |
| `join-self3` | 1.54 | 1.10 |
| `aggonce-cnt5` | 1.50 | 1.38 |
| `pol-threshold` | 2.07 | 1.94 |
| `aggonce-rate80` | 1.71 | 1.61 |
| `long-ev1000` | 1.29 | 1.20 |
| `join-both` (every predicate is read) | 1.05 | 1.07 |

`since-nu50`, `pol-sincefut` and `match-pseq` stay within the noise.

### The suite after the filter

Master and refine (1a7c483), one after the other on the container of this section
(`evaluation/perf/report0.md` and `report.md`):

| | master | refine |
|---|---|---|
| VeriMon timeouts (5 s) | 64 | **0** |
| both finish | 132 | 196 |
| geometric mean VeriMon/MonPoly | 2.71 | **1.13** |
| VeriMon faster / more than 2x slower | 0 / 103 | **61 / 3** |

MonPoly takes 0.88x its master time there, and the verified monitor 0.38x the original VeriMon's
over the 137 formulas that one finishes.  The three formulas still more than 2x slower are
`pol-sincefutb` (2.38), `pol-threshold` (2.07) and `aggonce-min50` (2.03); the next eight, all
between 1.84 and 1.99, are Since with a negated left operand, grouped aggregations over a window
and the regular expressions -- the same groups as above, and none of them the front end's.

### I: the grouped aggregation over a past window

The next round, I, takes the largest group: 14 of the 30 slowest formulas are a grouped
aggregation over a past window.  They are slow at a window of 5 as at a window of 50
(`aggonce-cnt5` 2.2x, `aggonce-cnt50` 1.8x in the suite), so what they pay for is per time point,
not per tuple of the window.  On `aggonce-max50` after 21.27, the result table is 20.5% of the
run and the per-group mapping's upkeep 19.8% as tuples enter and 19.2% as they leave.  21.22
priced an incremental result (only the groups that change) and declined it; I starts by
re-profiling the family on the filtered front end, and records here what it does.

## 21.29 I: the aggregation over a window, kept up in one pass per table

21.28 named the grouped aggregation over a past window as the next round: 14 of the 30 slowest
formulas, slow at a window of 5 as at 50.  Profiled on the filtered front end, `aggonce-cnt5`
(2.08 G instructions on the 1500-point log) spends its window's upkeep like this:

| step, per entering or leaving table | share |
|---|---|
| widening the entering and leaving tuples to the kernel's frame (`udagg_wide`), into a new tree each | 6.2% |
| selecting the tuples that newly enter or finally leave by their count (`ucnt_enter`, `ucnt_leave`), into a new tree each | 6.0% |
| the kernel's fold over those trees (`insert_maggaux`, `delete_maggaux`) | 11.4% |
| the separate passes that update the counts (`ucnt_add`, `ucnt_del`) | 10.9% |

The two intermediate trees exist only to be folded over once, and every tuple's count is read
twice, once to select it and once to update it.  21.22's price for an incremental *result* does
not apply here: this is the upkeep of the per-group mapping, which every time point does anyway.

`Dense_Agg_Upkeep.thy` does it in one pass over the table's sorted list, which reads each tuple's
count once, updates it, and hands the widened tuple straight to the kernel's step when the count
says it enters or leaves.  Three facts carry the proof, and the only one about aggregation was
already proved for the kernel:

* the kernel's steps commute (`insert_cnt_comm` and its five siblings, already proved for the
  kernel), so its `Finite_Set.fold` over the widened, filtered table is a fold over the table's
  list with the filter and the widening applied per tuple -- provided the list is distinct and
  the widening is injective on it (`fold_image_filter_list`);
* the widening `udwiden` is injective on the tuples of the length its mask expects
  (`inj_on_udwiden`) -- which the code checks, since the equation cannot assume it;
* the tuples of a table are distinct, so each tuple's count is unaffected by the updates of the
  tuples before it in the same pass (`cnt_ins_fold_eq`, `cnt_del_fold_eq`).

`uag_step_ins_eq` and `uag_step_del_eq` combine them: under the guard that the table is finite and
its tuples have the widening's length, the one pass computes exactly the counts and the aggregate
that the two passes did; otherwise the code takes the two passes.  `usa_add_new_table` and
`usa_expire` get code equations through the combined steps.  Nothing of the kernel's aggregation
structure, of the node's invariant or of the naturality proofs moves -- the change is code
equations proved against the ones they replace -- and the export differs from the previous one in
five new functions and the two window steps.

Instructions on the 1500-point log, and wall time on the suite's `base` log (median of five,
interleaved; ratio to MonPoly):

| formula | instructions | wall before → after | ratio before → after |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pol-threshold` | −13.0% | 1.21 s → 1.03 s | 2.05 → **1.74** |
| `aggonce-sum5` | −11.3% | 1.21 s → 1.10 s | 1.50 → 1.37 |
| `aggonce-cnt5` | −11.2% | 1.16 s → 1.00 s | 1.46 → 1.26 |
| `aggonce-cnt50` | −9.5% | | |
| `aggonce-max50` | −9.2% | 1.85 s → 1.65 s | 2.07 → **1.84** |
| `aggonce-min50` | | 1.90 s → 1.70 s | 2.21 → **1.99** |
| `aggonce-cnt500` | −7.4% | | |
| `aggonce-med20` | −5.9% | | |
| `aggonce-avg50` | −5.6% | | |
| `pol-sumfilter` | | 1.68 s → 1.48 s | 1.82 → 1.60 |
| `aggguard-sum` | | 1.42 s → 1.22 s | 1.64 → 1.42 |
| `aggonce-rate80` | | 2.74 s → 2.35 s | 1.69 → 1.44 |
| `onceex-50` (control) | 0.0% | | |
| `since-nu50` (control) | | 1.70 s → 1.75 s | 1.94 → 1.99 |

The saving is largest where the window is short or the result is filtered away, because there the
upkeep is the larger part of the run; MED and AVG gain least, because their per-group step (a rank
tree, a running sum and count) is itself the bigger cost.  The corpus is unchanged (601 same, 0
differing), the differential tests agree, and `dense_rank.t` covers the six aggregations over
sliding windows with repeated values.

## 21.30 The three slowest formulas: the trees' rebalancing, and a deletion the export lost

After 21.29 three formulas were still more than 2x MonPoly: `pol-sincefutb` (2.29), `aggonce-min50`
(2.11) and `since-nu5` (2.03).  Their instruction ratios are much lower (1.67, 1.34 and 1.57 on the
1500-point logs); the difference is memory, since `-verified` allocated 4.1 to 5.2 times MonPoly's
minor-heap words on all three.  An allocation profile (`MONPOLY_MEMPROF`) put **15% of all words
allocated, on each of them, on one line**: the red-black tree's `RBT_Impl.balance`, with most of
the next-largest sites in the same function.

**The rebalancing.**  `balance` restores the colour invariant at every level on the way back from
every insertion into every tree the monitor keeps.  Its equations are sequential, and the last one,
`balance a s t b = Branch B a s t b`, is the one that applies at almost every level.  The code
generator completes a sequential definition's patterns into disjoint nested ones, and in doing so
replaces the variables `a` and `b` of that last equation by the constructor applications they were
split into -- so the generated code rebuilds both subtrees from their fields, three blocks where
one is needed.  It is the same defect 21.27 removed from the rank trees, one layer further down,
and a case expression with overlapping nested patterns has it too: the case translation splits
bound variables and rebuilds them in the right-hand sides.

`Dense_RBT_Code.thy` gives `balance` a code equation that inspects its arguments one level at a
time -- a node, then its colour -- and never splits a variable its right-hand side uses; the two
checks for a red grandchild are two small functions, `bal_left` for the left side (only when it is
red) and `bal_right` for the right side.  The equation is proved against the library's six by a
case split.  The generated function falls through with `Branch (B, _, _, _, _) -> bal_right l k v
r` and builds nothing it does not have to.

**The lost deletion.**  21.8 gave the deletion of a key set from the Since structure's timestamp
mapping a code equation through the specialised deletion (`de_mapping_delete_set_nat`).  21.16's
second pass then made a second copy of the same operation at the same type from the generic
equations (`edl_mapping_delete_set`), and its redirection is the one the code took: all 13 call
sites went through the generic deletion with its comparator dictionaries, and the specialised copy
was not in the export at all.  The copy now has the specialised deletion for trees and the generic
equation for the other set representations.  None of the sweep's other duplicate copies had a
better hand-written equation to lose.

Instructions and minor-heap words on the 1500-point logs, and the wall-time ratio to MonPoly on the
suite's logs (median of five, interleaved):

| formula | instructions | allocation | ratio before → after |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pol-sincefutb` | −9.6% | −29.5% | 2.32 → **1.96** |
| `aggonce-min50` | −7.8% | −30.4% | 1.85 → **1.67** |
| `since-nu5` | −6.3% | −26.8% | 1.96 → **1.87** |
| `since-nu50` | | | 1.94 → 1.75 |
| `aggonce-cnt5` | −4.5% | −29.1% | 1.26 → 1.15 |
| `pol-threshold` | | | 1.66 → 1.49 |
| `A(x,y) AND ONCE[0,50] B(x,y)` | −4.1% | −23.2% | |
| `onceex-50` | −3.3% | −28.8% | 0.39 → 0.37 |
| `A(x,y) AND (C(x) UNTIL[0,20] B(x,y))` | −1.6% | −11.1% | |
| `match-pseq` | −1.2% | −10.1% | 1.78 → 1.80 |
| `join-both` | 0.0% | 0.0% | |

Of the two, the rebalancing is nearly all of it; the deletion alone was measured at −0.6% to −1.5%
on the Since formulas.  The allocation falls by up to 30% wherever trees are inserted into, which
is why the wall time moves by more than the instructions; `join-both`, whose result is built in one
pass without rebalancing, does not move.  None of the three is more than 2x MonPoly any more on these
measurements, and the suite rerun after this section (`evaluation/perf/report.md`) agrees: its
geometric mean went from 1.13 to 1.06 and no formula of it is left above 2x, the slowest being
`pol-sincefutb` 1.94, `pol-sincefut` 1.90 and `aggonce-min50` 1.74.  What is left of the three is what 21.27 and 21.22 priced: the negated
operand's deletions from four persistent trees on the burst log, and the aggregate's result table.
The Since node's garbage collection on a short window (11.6% of `since-nu5`: the index of the tuples
since the last match rebuilt from scratch, and the window's union built through the generic set
code) is recorded as a candidate, worth 3-5% there and little on longer windows.

The export differs from the previous one in `balance` and `edl_mapping_delete_set` and the two new
helpers; the corpus is unchanged (601 same, 0 differing) and the differential tests agree.

## 21.31 The match and since groups, and the suite's one verdict disagreement

After 21.30 the two groups furthest from MonPoly were `match` (geometric mean 1.39) and `since`
(1.25), and `match-pcnt` was the suite's one verdict disagreement.

### The final time point

For a formula that looks into the future the front end appends a last time point with the maximal
time stamp, which lets the verdicts still pending at the end of the log be decided.  For a
past-only formula it does not -- except for the regular expressions, because MonPoly's own regex
evaluation (`algorithm.ml`, `EMatchP`) finalises a time point only once the next one's time stamp
is known and needs the extra one to flush the last real time point.  The verified monitor outputs
a past-only formula's verdicts as the time points arrive, so the extra time point only added a
verdict about itself: `@MaxTS (time point 6000): (0)` on `match-pcnt`, a count over the empty group
that MonPoly never reports.  `main.ml` now drops the extra time point for every past-only formula
under `-verified` (5b9ef69).

Running the verified monitor with `-nonewlastts` altogether would be wrong in the other direction:
for a future formula neither monitor can decide the last verdicts without the final time point (on
`r <- CNT x EVENTUALLY[0,5] C(x)` the output would stop at time point 5993 instead of 5999), and
MonPoly adds it by default, so every future group would disagree.  The extra time point is harmless
to the verified monitor's guarantee: it is one more, empty, time point of the trace, and a future
formula's verdict at that time point is never decided.

### The regular-expression windows over short tuples

The dense match nodes of 21.24 kept the kernel's window at the node's own width.  Every firing
converted each operand table from short tuples back to boxed ones (`reopt_table`), widened it into
the node's columns (`dembedp`), ran the kernel's `update_matchP`/`update_matchF` over boxed tuples,
and stripped the option off the result again.  On the 1500-point `base` log that was, for
`match-pseq`, 40% of the run in the window and 18% in the conversions; for `match-pstar` 45% and
20%; for `match-fseq` 28% and 23%.

`Dense_Unbox.thy` already had the window this needed, written for the heap-resident monitor of
`Dense_Imp.thy` and never proved: `gupdate_matchP` and `gupdate_matchF`, polymorphic in the field
type, which probe each atom's table at the atom's own columns with the semi-join `dsemi` against a
mask, and intersect where the kernel joins two lax epsilons.  The dense nodes (`DMatchP`/`DMatchF`,
and the boxed `EDMatchP`/`EDMatchF`) now run it over boxed tuples on the operands at their own
frames, and the short-tuple nodes run it over their own tuples, so neither the conversions nor the
widening happen any more.

`Dense_Match_Gen.thy` proves that the generic window computes the narrowing of the kernel's:

* the semi-join against an atom is the narrowing of the kernel's join (`dsemi_join`, 20.2),
  and the join of two lax epsilons, both tables over the node's columns, is their intersection
  (`dtabc_join_same`);
* the unit table -- the tuple with every column blank -- is the empty short tuple, which is its
  narrowing only when the node has no columns; a strict epsilon returns it only in that case,
  because what it returns is a table over the node's columns (`table_reps_strict`), and
  `table_unit_table_Nil` reads the empty column list off that.

The past node keeps the relation it had (`dmrd_rel`, the window's entries narrowed), and
`dtabc_gupdate_matchP` is its step.  The future window also stores every undecided time point's
operand tables, which are now kept at the operands' frames, so `DsMatchF` carries a new relation,
`gmld_rel`, whose step and read are `dtabc_gupdate_matchF` and `dtabc_geval_matchF`.

Relating the short-tuple node to the boxed one is naturality under putting the option back on the
fields (`gupdate_matchP_reopt`, `gupdate_matchF_reopt`, `geval_matchF_reopt`), and `udeval_Rep` states
that for every state, well-formed or not.  That is why the window's atoms are now read totally
(`gnth`, the empty table past the end of the list): with the partial `!`, an out-of-range atom index
has no relation between the boxed and the short list.  A well-formed node never reads past the end.

| formula (instructions, `base` 1500) | before | after | MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|
| `match-pseq` | 1.815 G | 0.907 G (−50%) | 0.959 G |
| `match-pstar` | 2.471 G | 1.123 G (−55%) | 1.398 G |
| `match-p1` | 1.060 G | 0.826 G (−22%) | 0.853 G |
| `match-fseq` | 1.675 G | 0.894 G (−47%) | 1.000 G |
| `match-falt` | 1.690 G | 1.027 G (−39%) | 1.172 G |
| `match-f1` | 1.138 G | 0.863 G (−24%) | 0.953 G |

Wall time on the suite's `base` log (median of five, interleaved): `match-pseq` 0.77 s → 0.40 s,
`match-pstar` 1.05 s → 0.52 s, `match-p1` 0.48 s → 0.37 s, against MonPoly's 0.43 s, 0.62 s and
0.38 s.  The output is identical on the eleven regex formulas of the suite, and equal to MonPoly's.

### The Since join's deletions

When a Since node's left operand covers part of its columns, the join with it removes the tuples of
every index entry whose key fails the probe from the window's table and from the per-tuple time
stamps, for the tuples in the window and for those since the last match.  It gathered them into one
set first (`set_of_idx` over the failing entries) and subtracted that; `del_buckets` and
`del_buckets1` (`Dense_Idx_Code.thy`) fold over the failing entries and remove each entry's tuples in
turn, with code equations proved against the union (`fold_del_buckets`).  On `since-u50` (the
positive unary left operand) the instructions go from 1.679 G to 1.569 G (−6.6%), on `since-nu50`
(the negated one) from 2.453 G to 2.296 G (−6.4%); formulas whose left operand covers all the
columns take the other branch and do not move.

A first attempt kept the union and only specialised it -- `set_of_idx` unions through the generic,
dictionary-passing `sup` rather than the specialised `edl_union` -- and bought nothing (+0.3% and
−1.8%); the cost is the union's construction, not its dictionaries.  What remains of the `since`
group is what 21.27 priced: every time point inserts its tuples into five persistent trees and the
next join deletes most of them again, which MonPoly does on hash tables.

### The suite

The suite rerun after this section (`evaluation/perf/report.md`, df896bc) has no verdict
disagreement any more.  `match` went from 1.39 to 1.00, its eleven formulas between 0.80
(`match-pstar`) and 1.27 (`match-pnarrow`, whose operand is narrower than the node's frame); `since`
went from 1.25 to 1.23, with `since-u50` from 1.60 to 1.31 and `since-u5` from 1.43 to 1.25.  The
geometric mean over the 196 formulas stays at 1.06, with none above 2x: MonPoly ran 2% faster than
in the previous run, and the groups whose code did not change moved by up to 7% either way.  The
largest ratios left are `pol-sincefutb` (1.91), `since-nu5` (1.77), `since-nu500` (1.73) and
`aggonce-max50` (1.71): the negated left operand and the extremum's result table that 21.27 and
21.30 priced.

## 21.32 The Until group

After 21.31 `until` was the group furthest from MonPoly (`until-nu20` 1.61, the rest 1.22 to 1.31).
Its instruction ratios are lower than its wall-time ratios (`until-nu20` 2.35 G against MonPoly's
1.62 G), so part of the gap is allocation and collection rather than work.

### Dictionaries left in the window update

The profile still showed the Until window's inner operations passing type-class dictionaries:
the nested deque update `gupd_nested_step_dq` (16% of `until-nu20`), the merge of two time-stamp maps
(`Mapping.combine`, 7%) and the deletion of a key set from one (`mapping_delete_set`, 5%) -- all at
the time-stamp type `nat + nat` -- and the positive left operand's map was restricted with a generic
`Mapping.filter`.  The spec for `gupd_nested_step_dq` in `Dense_Code_Opt.thy` listed its type
arguments in the wrong order (`monomorphize` takes them in order of first occurrence in the type),
so its copy was never used; a second, equally wrong entry in `Dense_Mono_Sweep.thy` is gone.  The
merge and the deletion get copies at `nat + nat`, and `gadd_new_dq_code` (`Dense_Idx_Code.thy`)
restricts the map with `mfilter_keys`, which looks the table's tuples up.  Instructions on `base`
1500: `until-bb5` -2.4%, `until-u50` -2.3%, `until-nu20` -3.5% (b633493).

### Black heights

The library's join-based union, intersection and difference of red-black trees
(`RBT_Comparator_Impl`) compare the black heights of two trees at every join and at every recursive
step, and compute each as a `nat` by walking the left spine -- a boxed number per level per call,
5% of `until-nu20`.  `RBT_set_opt.thy` already decided these comparisons without counting for its
own trees; `Dense_RBT_Code.thy` restates the two comparisons (`bh_le_g`, `bh_lt_g`) at every value
type and gives the library's `small_rbt`, `flip_rbt`, the three joins and the union and
intersection code equations through them.  `bheight` no longer occurs in the export.  Instructions:
`until-nu20` -3.0%, `since-nu500` -1.2%, `until-u50` -1.0%, other set-heavy formulas 0 to -0.8%
(32a439a).

| formula (wall, `base`, median of five) | before | after | MonPoly | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `until-bb5` | 0.72 s | 0.67 s | 0.57 s | 1.27 → 1.18 |
| `until-bb50` | 0.77 s | 0.72 s | 0.62 s | 1.24 → 1.16 |
| `until-u5` | 0.62 s | 0.62 s | 0.52 s | 1.19 → 1.20 |
| `until-u50` | 0.72 s | 0.67 s | 0.57 s | 1.27 → 1.18 |
| `until-nu20` | 1.37 s | 1.27 s | 0.87 s | 1.58 → 1.46 |

The output is identical on the six formulas and on the test corpus (601 same).

### The window's result

At every time point that leaves the window the step rebuilt the verdict of the next one as
`(result - to_del) ∪ keys m'` (7.6% and 6.3% of `until-nu20`), and the insertion of every new
tuple also inserted it into `result` when it landed in the front entry of the nested deque (part
of the 6.4% in `edl_insert`).  The kernel's invariant (`valid_mmuaux'`, `Optimized_Until.thy`) says
that `result` is always the key set of that front entry.  The generic operations of
`Dense_Until_Gen.thy` no longer maintain the field: the step reports `Mapping.keys m` of the entry
it removes, which needs no comparisons, and the insertion folds over the table and the deque only.

The dense Until node was related to the kernel's structure by equality at the option instance.
It is now related through `fix_res`, which writes the front entry's key set into the field:
`geval_step_dq_eq`, `gshift_dq_eq`, `geval_dq_eq` and `gadd_new_dq_eq` state that the generic
operation followed by `fix_res` is the kernel's operation on the fixed state.  For the step that
is the observation that the merged front entry's keys are the surviving keys of the old front and
those of the next one, and it needs only the deques' shape; for the insertion it is that the
kernel's fold adds a tuple to the result exactly when it enters the front entry
(`fold_duntil_elem_step_dq_lift`).  The node's validity (`duxaux'`) is the old one of the fixed
state, so nothing above `Dense_Evt_Aux.thy` changed, and the short-tuple refinement
(`udeval_Rep`) kept its proof, with the accumulator of the insertion one component shorter.

| formula (instructions, `base` 1500) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `until-bb5` | 1.410 G | 1.368 G (-3.0%) |
| `until-bb50` | 1.491 G | 1.453 G (-2.6%) |
| `until-u5` | 1.317 G | 1.269 G (-3.6%) |
| `until-u50` | 1.456 G | 1.412 G (-3.0%) |
| `until-nu20` | 2.194 G | 1.960 G (-10.7%) |

Wall time on `until-nu20`, against the start of this section: 1.42 s → 1.12 s, against MonPoly's
0.87 s (ratio 1.63 → 1.29).  The output is identical on the six formulas and on the corpus.

### The suite

The suite rerun after this section (`evaluation/perf/report.md`, 701a880) puts `until` at 1.16,
from 1.31: the verified monitor takes 0.90x its previous time on the group, `until-nu20` went from
1.61 to 1.24, `until-bb50` from 1.29 to 1.06 and `until-u5` from 1.31 to 1.12, while `until-u50`
(1.22 to 1.27) stayed within the noise.  The geometric mean over the 196 formulas went from 1.06
to 1.05, with 74 formulas where the verified monitor is faster (from 68) and none above 2x;
MonPoly ran 3% slower than in the previous run.  The largest ratios left are `pol-sincefutb`
(1.90), `since-nu5` (1.89), `pol-threshold` (1.73) and `aggonce-max50` (1.71).

### What is left

The rest is what persistent trees cost: 6.5 million tuple comparisons (`edl_comparator` and
`comparator_event_data`, 13%), the lookups in the per-time-point maps of the nested deque (5%), and
`caml_modify` and the minor collector (8%), where MonPoly updates hash tables in place.

## 21.33 The next three worst ratios

After 21.32 the three largest ratios were `pol-sincefutb` (1.90), `since-nu5` (1.89) and
`pol-threshold` (1.73).

### The Since structure with an interval that contains zero

The Since structure keeps, besides the window, two mappings that record since when each tuple
has been seen (`tuple_since`, and its index `idx_since`), prunes them every so often (`dgc_msaux`,
which rebuilds the index and filters the mapping against every tuple of the window), and updates
both at every insertion and every join.  They are read at one place: when a time point moves from
`data_prev` -- the part of the window not yet old enough to count -- into `data_in`.  When the
interval contains zero no time point is too young, the insertion puts every table into `data_in`
directly, and `data_prev` stays empty; the two mappings are bookkeeping that nothing reads.  On
`since-nu5` they were a quarter of the run (the collection alone 11%).  Every Since of the suite
with a lower bound of zero pays for them: all of the `since` group but the ones with a positive
lower bound, and `pol-sincefutb`.

`Dense_Aux.thy` now has `dadd_new_table_lz` and `dgc_join_lz`, which skip the two mappings and the
collection when the interval contains zero and are the old operations otherwise, and the dense and
the short-tuple Since run them.  Their validity, `valid_dmsaux_lz`, is the verified one for *some*
choice of the two mappings and of the time of the last collection (`dset_since`), together with
`data_prev` being empty; the operations that do not touch the mappings commute with `dset_since`,
the join produces mappings of its own from the ones it is given, and the insertion of the old
operations is the new one with the mappings updated.  So `dsaux'` changed its validity and its
four lemmas their proofs, and nothing above `Dense_Aux.thy` changed.

| formula (instructions, `base`/`burst` 1500) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `since-nu5` | 2.103 G | 1.501 G (-28.6%) |
| `since-nu500` | 2.126 G | 1.531 G (-28.0%) |
| `pol-sincefutb` | 1.262 G | 0.946 G (-25.1%) |
| `since-u50` | 1.564 G | 1.208 G (-22.7%) |

MonPoly takes 1.475 G on `since-nu5`.

### The match of a predicate against its terms

`pol-threshold` counts over a past window, `r <- CNT y; x ONCE[0,60] A(x,y)`; inside the
aggregation the variables are renumbered, so the predicate is matched against its terms
(`umatch_table`) rather than copied, and that ran the generic image and `Option.these` with their
dictionaries: 9% of the run.  `umatch_table_rbt` (`Dense_Code_Opt.thy`) is one fold over the
tree that inserts the matches with the specialised insertion.  `pol-threshold` -5.7%,
`aggonce-max50` -3.6%; the rest of both is the window aggregate that 21.30 priced.

Wall time on the suite's logs (median of five): `since-nu5` 1.27 s → 0.87 s against MonPoly's
0.77 s (ratio 1.66 → 1.13), `pol-sincefutb` 1.82 s → 1.27 s (1.88 → 1.31), `since-nu500` 1.60 →
1.11, `since-u50` 1.33 → 1.00, `pol-threshold` 1.53 → 1.45.  The output is identical on these
formulas and on the corpus (601 same).

### The suite

The suite rerun after this section (`evaluation/perf/report.md`, 348bc53) puts the geometric mean
over the 196 formulas at 1.00, from 1.05, with 98 formulas where the verified monitor is faster
(from 74) and none above 2x.  The `since` group went from 1.23 to 0.94: `since-nu5` 1.89 to 1.22,
`since-nu500` 1.66 to 1.08, `since-u50` 1.50 to 1.04, and `pol-sincefutb` 1.90 to 1.33.  MonPoly
ran 2% slower than in the previous run.  The largest ratios left are the aggregations over a past
window: `aggonce-max50` (1.73), `aggonce-min50` (1.61) and `pol-threshold` (1.50).

## 21.34 N: the window's aggregate over the window's own tuples

After 21.33 the largest ratios were the aggregations over a past window, and 21.33's profile of
`aggonce-*` and `pol-threshold` put the gap in memory traffic more than in instructions: the
aggregate was still the kernel's `aggaux`, a mapping keyed by the *wide* tuple -- the group with a
hole at the result column, as an `event_data option list` -- so every tuple that entered or left
the window was widened to the kernel's frame, its group cut back off as a list of options, and the
result was a table of the kernel's frame that the node then narrowed again.

`Dense_Agg_Gen.thy` restates the kernel's structure over tuples of a bare element type: a mapping
from the group -- the tuple's suffix after the bound columns, no hole -- to a count, to a count and
a sum, or to the rank tree of the values (`'b gagg`), with the kernel's steps (`gi_cnt` ...
`gd_rank`), folds over the table's sorted list (`gins`, `gdel`), and a read that writes the
aggregate into each group (`gres`).  The window (`Dense_Evt_Agg.thy`) keeps it at `event_data
option`; the short-tuple window (`Dense_Unbox_Eval.thy`, `Dense_Unbox_UAgg.thy`) keeps it at
`event_data`.

* **Against the kernel.**  `Rk` maps the structure to the kernel's by renaming the keys with
  `kkey`, which puts the hole back (`kmap`, with `unkkey` as its partial inverse).  A kernel step
  on a widened tuple is the generic step on the tuple under `Rk` (`insert_cnt_kmap` and its five
  siblings); the kernel's `Finite_Set.fold` is the list fold because its steps commute
  (`fold_image_list`); and a step that fails drops the structure at once rather than carrying a
  flag (`fold_flag`).  So `insert_maggaux'` and `delete_maggaux'` on the widened table are `gins`
  and `gdel` under `Rk`, `init_maggaux'` is `ginit`, and `result_maggaux` is `gres` for keys of the
  group's length (`result_maggaux_Rk`).  The kernel's operations stay as the specification
  (`kag_*`); the window's invariant is the kernel's under `Rk` together with the keys being
  well-formed groups, and `ag_inv_init`, `ag_inv_ins`, `ag_inv_del` and `ag_res_inv` keep their
  statements, so nothing above `Dense_Evt_Agg.thy` on the dense side changed.
* **Against the short tuples.**  `rgagg` renames the keys by `reopt`, which the mapping
  operations commute with (`rmk`); the steps (`gstep_ins_rgagg`), the folds, the initial structure
  and the read (`gres_rgagg`) commute with it with no invariant, as the rest of the unboxed
  evaluator does.
* **The code.**  The entering and leaving tuples are the table's list filtered by the counts, so
  the one pass of 21.29 needs neither its guard nor the widening: `cnt_ins_fold` and
  `cnt_del_fold` hand each tuple to the generic step as they update its count.  The read walks the
  mapping's entries and inserts the node's tuple if it passes the node's guard (`ugmr_code`).  The
  steps are specialised at `event_data` (`edl_gi_cnt` ...), and the mappings at the two new value
  types (`nat × integer`, `list_aux`) get the short tuples' specialised operations; without the
  first, the generated steps built the list comparator at every lookup and the one pass was 5%
  *slower* than before.

Instructions on the suite's `base` log (1500 time points):

| formula | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `aggev-sum20` | 8.656 G | 7.884 G (−8.9%) |
| `pol-threshold` | 6.272 G | 5.758 G (−8.2%) |
| `aggonce-sum5` | 6.455 G | 5.949 G (−7.8%) |
| `aggonce-cnt50` | 8.313 G | 7.772 G (−6.5%) |
| `aggonce-max50` | 9.599 G | 9.042 G (−5.8%) |
| `aggonce-med20` | 15.066 G | 14.525 G (−3.6%) |

On `aggonce-cnt50` the window's upkeep went from 2.32 G to 2.07 G and the read from 2.01 G to 1.84 G,
and D1 read misses fell by 11% (measured before the steps were specialised); what is left of the read is the output itself, 1.2 million tuples
inserted into the result trees, which the previous code built as well.  MED gains least, because
its rank trees are the same as before.  Wall time on the suite's logs against the previous binary
(best of three, one worker): the `agg_once` family 0.91x, `agg_fut` 0.86x, `policy` 0.94x
(`pol-threshold` 0.86x, `pol-chain` 0.88x), with every verdict the same.  The corpus is unchanged
(601 same, 141 skipped, 0 differing) and `dune test` passes.

## 21.35 The policy group: a mapping's lookup and update in one descent

On the local runs after 21.34 the four largest ratios are in the `policy` group: `pol-threshold`
(1.48), `pol-sumfilter` (1.39), `pol-sincefutb` (1.25) and `pol-sincefut` (1.18); by group, `policy`
is at 1.02 and `once_scale` and `until` are the slowest (1.10, 1.09).  `pol-threshold`, a count over
`ONCE[0,60]` against a threshold, takes 5.76 G instructions under the verified monitor against
MonPoly's 5.09 G, only 1.13x, but its wall time is 1.48x: the difference is memory traffic.  D1
misses are 2.2x MonPoly's (61.5 M against 28.5 M), and they sit in the key comparisons and descents
of the two red-black trees that the window updates per tuple -- the count of every tuple in the
window (1.33 G of the 3.09 G the evaluator spends) and the aggregate of every group (0.64 G) --
where MonPoly uses hash tables.  Every update of an existing key there was a lookup followed by an
insertion: two descents of the same tree, comparing the same key with the same nodes.

`Dense_Map_Upd.thy` adds `mupd k F m = (lookup m k, update k (F (lookup m k)) m)` and gives it,
on a red-black tree, a code equation that does both in one descent (`rbt_comp_ins_lk`, proved
equal to the lookup paired with the insertion by induction over the insertion, with no invariant;
lifted to `mapping_rbt` with `code_dt`, and specialised at the short tuples' comparator and value
types).  The count set's insertion in the window of 21.29 and in the plain window (`ucnt_add1`,
`de_gcnt_add1`, `cnt_ins_fold`) and the aggregate's insertion steps (`gi_cnt`, `gi_sum`, `gi_rank`)
get code equations through it, proved against their definitions.  The deletions keep their lookup:
a deletion's rebalancing depends on whether the key goes, which is known only after the lookup.

| formula (instructions, `base`/`dom50` 1500) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `once-infnot` | 4.568 G | 4.345 G (−4.9%) |
| `pol-threshold` | 5.758 G | 5.558 G (−3.5%) |
| `aggonce-dom50` | 5.087 G | 4.946 G (−2.8%) |
| `aggonce-cnt50` | 7.772 G | 7.578 G (−2.5%) |
| `once-g50` | 6.089 G | 5.963 G (−2.1%) |
| `aggonce-max50` | 9.042 G | 8.871 G (−1.9%) |
| `since-bb500`, `since-nb5` (controls) | | 0.0% |

The saving is the lookups of the insertions: the entering tuples are mostly new, and each new
tuple and each touched group costs one descent less.  It is smaller than the traffic gap to
MonPoly, which is the price of comparison trees over short-tuple keys against hashing, and which
this change does not close.  The corpus is unchanged (601 same, 141 skipped, 0 differing) and
`dune test` passes; the container's wall times move by up to 15% per formula from run to run, so
the table gives instructions.

## 21.36 `pol-sincefutb` and `pol-sincefut`: the Since node's trees

The two formulas are `A(x,y) AND ((NOT C(x)) SINCE[0,b] B(x,y)) AND NOT EVENTUALLY[0,c] C(y)`, with a
Since whose left operand is negated and covers only `x`, so that the structure keeps an index from
`x` to the tuples it holds.  On `pol-sincefutb` (burst log) the verified monitor takes 7.84 G
instructions against MonPoly's 6.98 G (1.12x; `pol-sincefut` 7.26 G against 6.93 G, 1.05x) while
the local wall-time ratios are 1.25 and 1.18.  The Since node is 3.39 G of the evaluator's 4.12 G:

| step | share of the run |
|---|---|
| a new table: the index's buckets (`didx_add`) | 7.5% |
| a new table: the union into the window's table | 7.0% |
| a new table: the time stamps of the tuples (`upd_set`) | 6.5% |
| the join: dropping the buckets of the `C` tuples from the table and from the time stamps | 17.0% |

Two of these went through code that the earlier rounds had not specialised.  The union and the
difference of two trees (`rbt_comp_union_swap_rec`, `comp_minus`), on a small operand, insert or
delete its keys one at a time and split the other tree at a key, and they took the comparator as an
argument and called it through a closure at every node; `Dense_Map_Upd.thy` gives them copies at
the short tuples' comparator with the specialised insertion, deletion and split (proved by unfolding
the definitions).  And a tuple entering the index is added to its key's bucket by a lookup and an
update of the index, which `mupd` of 21.35 does in one descent (`de_didx_add1_mupd`).

| formula (instructions, 1500 time points) | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| `pol-sincefutb` | 7.838 G | 7.543 G (−3.8%) |
| `since-nu5` | 5.930 G | 5.831 G (−1.7%) |
| `pol-sincefut` | 7.258 G | 7.148 G (−1.5%) |
| `since-nb5` | 5.705 G | 5.652 G (−0.9%) |
| `since-bb500` | 4.275 G | 4.259 G (−0.4%) |
| `once-g50`, `pol-threshold`, `join-both` (controls) | | 0.0% |

What is left of the gap is not in instructions.  About a fifth of the run is the garbage
collector -- minor-heap promotion, marking and the write barrier of the trees' rebuilt paths --
which MonPoly's mutable hash tables avoid, and neither a larger minor heap nor a lower space
overhead changes the wall time by more than 3%; the front end (lexer, `List.assoc_opt` in
`Log_parser.parse_db`, `List.mem` in `Filter_rel.rel_OK`) costs both systems the same.  The corpus is
unchanged (601 same, 141 skipped, 0 differing) and `dune test` passes.

## 21.37 The Until group: the add step's per-tuple work

On the Until formulas (1500 time points of `base.log`) the verified monitor took 5.0 to 7.8 G
instructions against MonPoly's 4.5 to 6.4 G, with `until-nu20` (`B(x,y) AND ((NOT C(x))
UNTIL[0,20] A(x,y))`) the worst at 1.21x.  The add step (`gadd_new_dq`) was two thirds of the
evaluator, and most of it was per tuple of the right operand:

| step | `until-nu20` share of the run |
|---|---|
| the tuple's entry in the deque's time stamp map: lookup, then update (`gupd_nested_step_dq`) | 17.1% |
| the tuple into the step's table (`Set.insert`) | 5.2% |
| the interval's left end at the tuple's time point: a walk of the time stamp deque (`P`) | 4.9% |
| the step's expiry: the tuples to delete, filtered, then deleted with the generic deletion | 11.2% |
| the left operand's map, for a positive left operand: filter, difference, insertions | (5.9% on `until-bb50`) |

`Dense_Map_Upd.thy` gives the monomorphised step code equations for each, all proved by unfolding
the definitions and the facts named:

* the time stamp map's entry is `mupd` of 21.35, one descent (`de_gupd_nested_step_dq_mupd`), and
  the expiry's deletion is the specialised one of the Since structure at the time stamps' value type
  (`de_mapping_delete_set_tstp_rbt`);
* for an interval that starts at 0 the left-end test always holds (`memL_mono`), and every tuple of
  the right operand then enters the step's table, which is the operand's table itself: the fold keeps
  only the deque (`guntil_a2_step`, `fold_guntil_elem_step_dq_True`, `de_gadd_new_dq_code`);
* the expiry folds a conditional deletion (`mdel_if`) over the leaving tables' tuples instead of
  filtering them into a set first; the fold looks each tuple up in the map it has built so far, which
  is the original map at the tuples not yet seen, since the list is distinct (`lookup_fold_mdel_if`,
  `de_geval_step_dq_code`);
* the positive left operand's map, restricted to the latest left table with the new tuples at the
  current time point, is the table's own tree with the values filled in by a lookup (`mrefresh`,
  `RBT_Mapping2.map`), with no insertion and no rebalancing.

| formula (instructions, 1500 time points) | before | after | MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|
| `until-nu20` | 7.755 G | 6.680 G (−13.9%) | 6.429 G |
| `until-u50` | 5.588 G | 4.955 G (−11.3%) | 4.806 G |
| `until-u5` | 4.999 G | 4.336 G (−13.3%) | 4.534 G |
| `until-bb5` | 5.394 G | 4.228 G (−21.6%) | 5.041 G |
| `until-bb50` | 5.759 G | 4.620 G (−19.8%) | 5.223 G |
| `until-bb500` | 5.656 G | 4.543 G (−19.7%) | 5.325 G |
| `since-nu5`, `pol-sincefutb`, `once-rate80` (controls) | | 0.0% | |

The instruction ratio of the group goes from 1.03–1.21 to 0.84–1.04.  `once_scale` (1.07 in the
local run) is not in instructions: `once-rate80` executes 11.73 G against MonPoly's 11.33 G
(1.035), and the collector executes fewer instructions than MonPoly's (2.78 G against 3.02 G).  The
rest of the wall-time gap is the first-level data cache: 124 M misses against 86 M, of which 64 M
against 34 M are writes -- the fresh lines of the red-black trees' rebuilt paths, which every update
of the window's count set allocates and MonPoly's hash tables update in place.  A code equation does
not change that; a structure that allocates less per update would.  The corpus is unchanged (601 same, 141
skipped, 0 differing) and `dune test` passes.

## 21.38 `pol-threshold` and `aggonce-min50`/`max50`: the per-tuple descents

On `pol-threshold` (`(r <- CNT y; x ONCE[0,60] A(x,y)) AND 40 < r`) the verified monitor executed
5.56 G instructions against MonPoly's 5.09 G (1.09x), on `aggonce-min50` and `aggonce-max50` 8.8 G
against 7.8 G (1.13x); the local wall-time ratios are 1.40 and 1.25.  Most of the node's work is per
tuple: a tuple entering the window costs about 3 900 instructions and one leaving it about 5 000,
because the window's count set is read and then written (a lookup, then a deletion or an update)
and so is the aggregate's map from groups -- four descents of a red-black tree for a tuple leaving.
And a key already in a tree, which is what the aggregate's map almost always holds, is updated by
the insertion, which rebalances the path it rebuilds although the tree's shape does not change.

`Dense_Map_Upd.thy` removes the extra descents:

* `rbt_comp_del_lk` is the deletion returning the value it removed, in the deletion's one descent
  (proved equal to the lookup and the deletion by induction along the deletion), lifted to the
  trees with the red-black invariant (`del_lk`) and to the mappings (`mdel_lk`).  The count sets'
  deletions (`ucnt_del1`, the window's `gcnt_del1` and the aggregate window's `cnt_del_fold`) go
  through it: a tuple whose count is one, the common case, costs one descent, and a count above one
  an update of the original map.
* The aggregate's decrements (`gd_cnt`, `gd_sum`) read and write the group's entry by `mupd` of
  21.35, one descent, and delete the group only when its last tuple leaves.
* `rbt_comp_ins_lk` of 21.35 no longer rebalances when the key is present: it only replaces the
  value, which is the tree's `map_entry` (`rbt_comp_ins_lk`: the result is the insertion for a new
  key and `rbt_comp_map_entry` for a present one), so the lifted `upd_lk` keeps the invariant and
  `mupd` the mapping.  This one needs the invariant, since the insertion recolours a black node with
  two red children even when the key is present; the lifting supplies it.

| formula (instructions) | before | after | MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pol-threshold` | 5.558 G | 5.324 G (−4.2%) | 5.089 G |
| `pol-sumfilter` | 8.082 G | 7.876 G (−2.5%) | 7.032 G |
| `aggonce-min50` | 8.818 G | 8.680 G (−1.6%) | 7.782 G |
| `aggonce-max50` | 8.873 G | 8.749 G (−1.4%) | 7.808 G |
| `once-rate80` | 11.729 G | 11.530 G (−1.7%) | 11.334 G |
| `until-nu20`, `since-nu5` | | −0.7%, −0.4% | |

What is left on the aggregations over a past window is the result: at every time point the node
builds its table of groups afresh, one insertion per group (20% of `aggonce-min50`, 10% of
`pol-threshold`, where the constraint `40 < r` keeps few of them), while from one time point to the
next only the groups the entering and leaving tuples touch can change -- and for MIN and MAX few of
those do.  The corpus is unchanged (601 same, 141 skipped, 0 differing) and `dune test` passes.

## 21.39 The aggregations over a past window: the result table, and a guard that did not reach the node

**MIN and MAX keep their result table.**  21.38 ended on the result read: at every time point the
aggregation over a past window built its table of groups afresh, one insertion per group, although
only the groups touched by the entering and leaving tuples can change.  `Dense_Agg_Cache.thy`
keeps the table next to the window's count set and aggregate (`ucache`, a typedef whose invariant
says that the table is the aggregate's result whenever the aggregate is there) and updates it
tuple by tuple: `cupd` removes the group's old result tuple and inserts the new one, and does
nothing when the group's value did not change.  The window's operations (`usc_*`) are the old
ones (`usa_*`) with the table threaded through, proved equal to them under the projection `ucs`
that forgets it, so the unboxed window's representation function only gains that projection and
nothing above it moves.

Measured on every aggregation it lost on `CNT` and `SUM`: their value changes with almost every
tuple, so the table is updated per tuple where it was rebuilt per group, and per time point there
are more tuples than groups.  For MIN and MAX the group's value changes rarely.  The table is
therefore kept for those two only (`cachedp`); for the others the node reads the result as before.

**The rank steps return the old and the new value.**  A table update needs the group's value
before and after the step.  Read off the aggregate's mapping that is two more descents per tuple
(`edl_gval`, 6.6% of `aggonce-min50`).  `Dense_Map_Upd.thy` adds `rbt_comp_ins_lk2`, the update
descent of 21.35 that also returns the value it wrote (proved to be the old descent with `F`
applied to the value found), lifted to `upd_lk2` and `mupd2`.  The rank steps (`gstepv_ins`,
`gstepv_del`) go through it and return the group's multiset before and after, from which the
result value is read (`rkval`); the table update then costs no lookup.  The rank deletion
(`gd_rank`) also goes through `mupd2` now, one descent where it was a lookup and an update, and
descends again only to delete a group whose multiset became empty.

**A comparison that stopped above an anti-join.**  `pol-sumfilter` is
`(r <- SUM y; x ONCE[0,60] A(x,y)) AND NOT C(x) AND 4000 < r`.  21.21's guard puts a comparison
into the aggregation over a window when the comparison is conjoined to it directly; here the front
end's `convert_multiway` makes of the formula `(C(x) negated, the aggregate) AND 4000 < r`, a
comparison above a multiway conjunction, so the node built all ~200 groups at every time point,
anti-joined them with `C` and filtered them afterwards (the read and the anti-join were 20% and
13% of the run).  `Dense_Push.thy`'s rewrite (21.31, `push_ex`) now also moves such a comparison
onto the first operand of the multiway conjunction that is an aggregation over a window and binds
the comparison's variables (`and_cstr`, `ands_cstr`), where it becomes the guard.  The rewrite
keeps everything the monitor's specification depends on -- free variables, satisfaction, progress,
relevant events, safety, future-boundedness and typing (`*_and_cstr`) -- so
`dmonitor_mverdicts_push` covers it unchanged.  The move is made onto window aggregations only:
onto another operand it trades filtering the join's result for filtering the operand, which pays
off only when the operand is the smaller of the two -- `aggguard-cnt`, whose operand is a plain
aggregation over all of `B`, was 2.7% slower with the move and is unchanged without it.

| formula (instructions) | 21.38 | after | MonPoly | ratio |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `aggonce-min50` | 8.680 G | 7.490 G (−13.7%) | 7.795 G | 1.115 → **0.961** |
| `aggonce-max50` | 8.749 G | 7.550 G (−13.7%) | 7.850 G | 1.120 → **0.962** |
| `aggonce-min5` | 6.055 G | 6.001 G (−0.9%) | 6.233 G | 0.963 |
| `pol-sumfilter` | 7.876 G | 5.602 G (−28.9%) | 7.035 G | 1.120 → **0.796** |
| `aggguard-sum` | 7.242 G | 6.808 G (−6.0%) | 6.459 G | 1.054 |
| `pol-threshold` | 5.324 G | 5.317 G (−0.1%) | 5.091 G | 1.044 |
| `aggonce-cnt50`, `aggonce-sum50`, `aggonce-med20`, `agg-min`, `aggguard-cnt`, `pol-p3` | | ±0.1% | | |

`pol-threshold` (`CNT`, guard already in place since 21.21) is where 21.38 left it.  What remains
there is per tuple: a tuple entering the window costs a descent of the count set, keyed by the
whole tuple, and one of the aggregate's map, keyed by its group, and leaving costs the same again;
MonPoly does the same work in two hash tables.  Keeping the count set per group would save the
second descent's comparisons at the price of restating the count set and its naturality proofs;
it is not done here.  The corpus is unchanged (601 same, 141 skipped, 0 differing) and `dune test`
passes.

## 21.40 The count set by group, for the counts and the sums over a past window

21.39 ended on `pol-threshold` (`(r <- CNT y; x ONCE[0,60] A(x,y)) AND 40 < r`): a tuple entering
or leaving the window costs a descent of the count set, keyed by the whole tuple, and one of the
aggregate's map, keyed by its group.  Both trees are large, and the second descent repeats the
first one's comparisons on the group's columns; the misses of the two dominate the node.

**The count set by group.**  `Dense_Agg_Cache.thy` now represents the window's count set and
aggregate, for `CNT`, `SUM` and `AVG`, as one map from each group to its summary (the count, or the
count and the sum) together with the count set of the group's tuples (`'a nmap`, `ucrep`).  The
flat count set and the aggregate are its projections (`nflat`, `nsumm`); a tuple's step (`nins`,
`ndel`) is proved to project to the flat step (`nflat_nins`, `nsumm_nins`, `nflat_ndel`,
`nsumm_ndel`), under an invariant that the summary's count is the size of the group's set and that
each set holds only its group's tuples (`ninv`).  `ucache` keeps the representation in place of the
flat pair; its interface (`uc_cnt`, `uc_ax`, `uc_step_ins`, `uc_step_del`, `uc_res`) and the lemmas
about it are unchanged, so nothing above it moves.  A sum over a value that is not an integer goes
back to the flat representation, as the flat aggregate does.  In code a step descends the outer
map once with `mupd` or `mupd2` (21.35, 21.39) and the group's small map once, and deletes the group
in a second descent only when its last tuple leaves (`nins_mupd`, `ndel_mupd2`); the result is read
off the outer map directly (`uc_res_code`), and the mapping operations at the two new value types
are specialised like the others.

**The key of a step.**  The first build kept the flat representation on `pol-threshold`: the
step compared the whole key the cache was made for, and the constraint on the result (`40 < r`) is
pushed into the node after the cache is made, so the first step found a different key and went back
to the flat pair.  For the aggregations that keep no result table the representation depends only
on the group arity, which is what the step compares now.

| formula (instr. + 10·D1 miss + 100·LL miss) | before | after | MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pol-threshold` | 10.03 G | 8.73 G (−13.0%) | 6.72 G |
| `pol-sumfilter` | 10.50 G | 9.34 G (−11.0%) | 9.64 G |
| `aggonce-cnt50` | 13.61 G | 12.37 G (−9.1%) | 11.54 G |
| `aggonce-sum50` | 13.37 G | 12.18 G (−8.9%) | 11.59 G |
| `aggonce-min50` | 14.57 G | 14.58 G | 9.84 G |

Instructions alone fall by 11% on `pol-threshold` (5.07 G to 4.50 G, MonPoly 5.10 G) and by 8% on the
`aggonce` counts and sums.  A prototype written directly on the trees reached 7.95 G on
`pol-threshold`; the rest is the mapping layer's per-operation overhead (the comparator check, the
lifting's boxes), a few percent spread thinly.  The corpus is unchanged (652 same, 149 skipped, 0
differing), `dune test` passes, and on a random log the grouped and ungrouped `CNT`, `SUM` and `AVG`
over integer and float values, with and without a constraint, agree with the previous build and
with MonPoly.

## 21.41 The deletion's rebalancing, without rebuilding what it looks at

On `pol-sincefutb` the verified monitor allocates 357 M words against MonPoly's 123 M, and the
estimate's gap (15.3 G against 10.0 G) is mostly the last-level write misses of that allocation
(47 M against 15 M).  A little over half of it is the front end (the log's lexer and parser and the
conversion into tables, which alone allocate more than all of MonPoly); about a third is the Since
node's trees, and of that the deletions -- from the table and the time stamps when a `C` tuple
drops its bucket, and from the count sets -- are the larger part.

21.30's reason for rewriting the insertion's `balance` holds for the deletion too: its steps
`balance_left`, `balance_right` and `combine`, and the specialised deletions' steps to the left and
to the right (`edl_rbtc_del_from_left`, `edl_rbtc_del_lk_fl` and their mirror images), are
sequential equations, and the code generator's completed patterns rebuild the subtrees they only
inspect -- a black node passed on unchanged, rebuilt at every level of every deletion before the
recursive call.  `Dense_RBT_Code.thy` gives them equations that inspect one level at a time and
never split a variable that the right-hand side uses (`balance_left_code`, `balance_right_code`,
`combine_code`, `edl_rbtc_del_from_left_code`, ..., each proved by cases on the original
equations).  Two smaller ones go with them: `bal_right` evaluated its fallback before looking at
the case that drops it, and `paint` rebuilt the root on every insertion and deletion although it
almost always has the colour already.

| formula (instr. + 10·D1 miss + 100·LL miss) | before | after | MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|
| `pol-sincefutb` | 15.30 G | 14.71 G (−3.9%) | 10.03 G |
| `pol-sincefut` | 14.59 G | 13.80 G (−5.4%) | 9.70 G |
| `since-nu5` | 11.28 G | 10.69 G (−5.3%) | |
| `until-nu20` | 12.80 G | 12.35 G (−3.5%) | |
| `once-rate80` | 23.34 G | 22.54 G (−3.4%) | 15.75 G |
| `since-bb500` | 7.64 G | 7.43 G (−2.8%) | 7.14 G |
| `aggonce-min50` | 14.58 G | 14.19 G (−2.7%) | 9.84 G |
| `pol-threshold` | 8.73 G | 8.68 G (−0.6%) | 6.72 G |
| `join-both` | 5.21 G | 5.20 G | 3.96 G |

What remains of the Since node's share is the trees' path copying itself: a tuple entering costs
insertions into the table, the index's bucket and the time stamps, and a dropped bucket costs a
deletion from the table and from the time stamps per tuple.  Keeping the time stamps in the index's
buckets would save one of the three trees, at the price of restating the Since structure and its
proofs.  The corpus is unchanged (652 same, 149 skipped, 0 differing) and `dune test` passes.

## 21.42 The imperative refinement removed

Since 21.26 the binary ships only the export of `MFODL_Monitor_Dense.Monitor_Code_Dense`, which
imports nothing of Imperative HOL: the session `MFODL_Monitor_Imp` (the heap-resident Since and
Until structures, tables and joins, and the unverified heap-resident dense monitor `Dense_Imp`)
and the theory `Imperative_Join` of `MFODL_Monitor_Devel` (a hash-table probe join selected by a
run-time flag that nothing set) were no longer reachable from it.  Both are removed, with the
sessions only they needed (`HOL-Imperative_HOL`, `Separation_Logic_Imperative_HOL`,
`Collections`, `Automatic_Refinement`, `Native_Word`) and the micro-benchmark of their tables
(`evaluation/table_bench`); they remain in the history before this entry.  Two adaptations of the generated
code had come in with those libraries and are restated in `Monomorphic_Code` (the direct identity
for `literal_string8`, and `Automatic_Refinement`'s unfolding of `set xs ∪ set ys` into
`set (xs @ ys)`), and two proofs there that had used simp rules of theirs are given the rules
they need; the export is unchanged but for the flag `Config.imperative_join`, which nothing read.  The earlier entries'
references to them describe the code as it was then.  The command-line flags that once selected
the heap structures (`-heap_since`, `-heap_maps`, `-heap_join_min`, `-bulk_min`,
`-imperative_join`) were already accepted and ignored, and stay so.

## 21.43 A frozen predicate taken out of the windows, and the freeze bound with `LET`

The monitor evaluates `FRZ q = φ IN ψ` by replaying `ψ` over the whole prefix at every time
point, with `q` fixed to `φ`'s value there: quadratic in the prefix.  The `frz` family ran
0.4 to 17 s against MonPoly's 0.03 to 1 s.  A new theory `Dense_Frz` rewrites the formula before
the dense state is built (`udminit_push φ = udminit_safe (push_ex (frz_opt φ))`):

* `hoist` takes a conjunct `q(…)` of the frozen predicate out of the right operand of a Since
  whose interval contains 0 (`ONCE[0,b]`, `S[0,b]`), of an Until (`EVENTUALLY`, `U`) and of a
  Next: `φ S[0,b] (α ∧ q)` becomes `q ∧ φ S[0,b] α`.  The frozen predicate does not change over
  time, so the satisfaction relation is the same; the progress is too, since the frozen
  predicate's progress is always either the whole prefix or nothing (`progress_hoist`).  Prev
  and a Since with a lower bound are left alone: there the original has a verdict at the first
  time point without the frozen predicate and the rewritten formula does not.
* When afterwards the frozen predicate occurs under no temporal operator (`now_only`), the
  freeze is a `LET` (`sat_Frz_now_only`, `progress_Frz_now_only`), which the monitor evaluates
  incrementally.
* The conjunct is also found under an existential quantifier whose variable it does not
  mention (`EXISTS y. A(x,y) AND q(x)`); taken out, its variables are renumbered (`dec_trm`).
* `frz_opt` applies the rewrite only when its result is monitorable, so safety need not be
  shown preserved.  Formulas have no executable equality, so the conjunct is taken out of a
  list of conjuncts by its position (`frz_find`).

`dmonitor_mverdicts_push` now states the verdicts of the monitor started on
`push_ex (frz_opt φ)`.  On the `frz` family (seconds; old binary, new, MonPoly):

| formula | before | after | MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|
| frz-now | 0.40 | 0.03 | 0.03 |
| frz-once20 | 0.64 | 0.09 | 0.08 |
| frz-ev5 | 1.14 | 0.06 | 0.06 |
| frz-since | 0.70 | 0.05 | 0.10 |
| frz-nested | 16.66 | 0.04 | 0.98 |
| frz-len1000 | 2.78 | 0.19 | 0.15 |
| frz-outer | 0.61 | 0.05 | 0.05 |
| frz-oncelb | 0.59 | 0.59 | 0.06 |
| frz-prev | 0.44 | 0.39 | 0.05 |

The outputs are identical on the family and on the 652 `-verified` commands of the test corpus.

## 21.44 The right operand of a release, evaluated once

The monitor evaluates `φ R[a,b] ψ` by the formula that defines it through the past and future
operators, and that formula has four or five copies of `ψ`.  Each copy is its own subformula,
so each is evaluated.  VeriMon's rewriting turns `C(x) AND ALWAYS[0,5] (EXISTS y. A(x,y) OR B(x,y))`
(`der-guardalw`) into a release, and the multiway conversion then expands it into
`(ψ U ANDS[ψ; FF]) OR ((ψ U PREV ψ) OR (ψ U ANDS[ψ; NEXT TT]))`, which evaluates the quantified
disjunction four times per time point.

A new theory, `Dense_Share`, binds the operand with `LET` to a fresh predicate
`__release(x₀,…)` and releases that predicate: `share_rw` rewrites `α AND (φ R ψ)` to
`LET __release = ψ IN α AND (φ R __release(…))`, and a bare `φ R ψ` the same way.  The rewrite
fires only when `ψ` is not already a predicate, when its free variables are exactly
`0..nfv ψ - 1` (the predicate's arguments), and when the name is not used anywhere in the formula.
`share_opt` keeps the result only if it is monitorable.  The proofs are congruences:
satisfaction, progress, free variables and typing are all unchanged (`sat_share_rw`,
