`progress_share_rw`, `wty_share_rw`).

In the first version the rewrite sat in `udminit_push` alone, which runs after the multiway
conversion.  By then there is no release left, so it never fired.  `udminit_verimon` now
applies `share_opt` to the prepared formula before `convert_multiway`
(`ψ_mw mw = (if mw then convert_multiway (share_opt ψ) else ψ)`), and the lemmas of `ψ_mw`
carry over through `verimon_spec_share_opt` and `safe_share_opt_ψ`.

| formula | before (s) | after (s) | MonPoly (s) | Ir before | Ir after | Ir MonPoly |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| der-guardalw | 1.59 | 0.82 | 0.68 | 11.58G | 5.72G | 4.85G |
| der-hist5 | 0.58 | 0.39 | not monitorable | | | |
| der-always5 | 0.67 | 0.44 | not monitorable | | | |
| der-guardhist | 0.79 | 0.76 | 0.70 | | | |

## 21.45 The aggregation outside a window, without the multiset

An aggregation that is not over a window (`uagg`) clusters its operand's table by group.  It
then clusters each group again by the aggregated value to count the multiplicities, flattens
that multiset into a sorted list, and folds over the list.  On `agg-nested`
(`s <- MAX r (r <- CNT y; x ONCE[0,10] A(x,y))`) the outer MAX spent half the run there
(4.8G of 9.9G instructions): RBT insertions of whole tuples, comparing lists.

Two code equations for `uagg` replace its definition:

* MIN and MAX (`Dense_Map_Upd`, `uagg_code`): one pass over the table's sorted list keeps, per
  group, the extreme seen so far (`mm_step`, one descent through `mupd`).  The order on
  `event_data` is total only within one kind of value, and not on floats.  So the pass runs
  only when every value is an integer; it folds `max`/`min` on `integer`, and the result is
  wrapped back in `EInt`.  `uagg_mm_gen` proves the result equal to `uagg`'s from
  `flatten_uagg_values`: the flattened multiset of a finite, non-empty group lists exactly
  that group's values.  Otherwise the code falls back to `uagg`'s own equation (`uagg_gen`).
* The other aggregations (`Dense_Agg_Code`, `uagg_g_code`) build the window aggregate's structure
  (`gagg`: counts, count and sum, or an order-statistic tree per group) from scratch with
  `gins` and read the result with `gres`.  `uagg_gins` gets the equality from the window's
  invariant (`ag_inv_ins`, `ag_res_inv`) through the representation map `rgagg`, when all the
  tuples have one width that the parameters fit (`dagg_ok`, checked at run time).  An
  aggregation the structure cannot represent (`ginit ω = None`, e.g. a SUM of floats) goes to
  `uagg_gen` directly.

The structure was tried for MIN/MAX first and lost: the order-statistic tree's insertions
cost more than the two clusterings (agg-nested +2.9%).

| agg-max | 0.49 | 0.38 | 0.51 | 3.83G | 3.12G | 4.00G |
| agg-min | 0.49 | 0.38 | 0.50 | 3.84G | 3.12G | 4.00G |
| agg-nogroup | 0.42 | 0.28 | 0.38 | 3.30G | 2.48G | 3.05G |
| agg-cnt | 0.49 | 0.44 | 0.48 | 3.91G | 3.38G | 3.97G |
| agg-sum | 0.48 | 0.42 | 0.48 | 3.85G | 3.33G | 4.00G |
| agg-med | 0.63 | 0.53 | 0.62 | 4.86G | 4.39G | 4.76G |
| aggguard-cnt | | | | 5.01G | 4.47G | 4.56G |
| agg-since | | | | 6.02G | 5.42G | 6.28G |
| agg-float | | | | 5.09G | 5.09G | 4.75G |

`-verified` commands of the test corpus.  The cram tests pass.

## 21.46 `once-inf`: not done, and why

`A(x,y) AND ONCE[0,*) B(x,y)` runs 1.14 s against MonPoly's 0.87 s, and `inf-agg` 1.17 s
against 1.02 s.  The instruction counts are close (5.04G against 4.88G for once-inf), but the
verified monitor's heap peaks at 9.4M words against MonPoly's 1.2M, and its last-level cache
misses are twice MonPoly's.  The Since structure keeps each time point's table in its
`data_in` queue until the table leaves the window.  With no right bound, no table ever leaves,
so the queue holds the whole history even though the set of distinct tuples stays small.
Dropping the tables when the interval has no right bound would need a new representation
relation for the Since structure at all three levels (kernel, dense, short tuples).  Today
that relation is a function of the state, and this change would make it non-injective.  Left
for later.

## 21.47 `frz-oncelb` and `frz-prev`: the frozen conjunct out of a lower-bounded Since and a Prev

MonPoly evaluates `FRZ q = φ IN ψ` by starting a fresh copy of ψ at every time point, with q
bound to φ's value there (`FrzPerInst` in `algorithm.ml`).  What makes that fast is its "fix #1":
`past_reach ψ` sums the upper bounds of ψ's past operators (ONCE, SINCE, PREV, …; the future
operators add nothing, since their future side is served by the drive's look-ahead).  When the
sum d is finite, each copy is replayed only over the time points in `[τ_i − d, τ_i]` instead of the
whole prefix.  The cost per time point is the window, not the prefix.  VeriMon's FRZ node
(`MFrz`/`UDFrz`) has no such bound: each new frozen value replays ψ over the prefix of length j
(`freeze_meval0` with `replicate j α`).

21.43 avoided the replay where it could by hoisting the frozen conjunct out of the temporal
operators and binding the freeze with `LET`.  It left out a Since whose interval misses 0 and
a Prev.  There the original formula has a verdict at time point 0 (empty) before φ's value at 0
is known, and the hoisted `q ∧ …` does not, so the progress differs.  The FRZ progress puts the
frozen predicate's progress at j before φ's progress and at 0 after it.  So this can only happen
when φ lags behind the prefix.  When it never does, the only i at which the frozen predicate
counts as unavailable is i = j, where both formulas have progress at most j anyway.

`hoist` now takes a flag, set when the frozen formula never lags (`full_now`).  A formula
qualifies when it is built from the past operators, the connectives, the quantifiers and the
aggregations over predicates that no enclosing `LET`/`FRZ` binds.  `frz_rw` carries the list of
those bound names.  With the flag, the conjunct also comes out of a Since with any interval and
out of a Prev.  `progress_frz_rw` now assumes that every unbound predicate is known at all of the
prefix or at none of it (`full_env`), which the top level (`Map.empty`) satisfies.  On the future
side nothing was missing: Until (and so EVENTUALLY) and Next were already hoisted for every
interval.  The suite gains `frz-sincelb`, `frz-evlb`, `frz-untillb` and `frz-lagphi`.

| formula | before (s) | after (s) | MonPoly (s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| frz-oncelb | 0.69 | 0.08 | 0.07 |
| frz-prev | 0.49 | 0.04 | 0.05 |
| frz-sincelb | 1.25 | 0.07 | 0.08 |
| frz-evlb | 0.08 | 0.08 | 0.07 |
| frz-untillb | 0.05 | 0.05 | 0.13 |
| frz-lagphi | 1.77 | 1.73 | 0.11 |

`frz-lagphi` (`FRZ q(x) = EVENTUALLY[0,3] C(x) IN ONCE[2,10] (…)`) is the case that remains: the
frozen formula lags, the original really does output time point 0 before it, and the replay
stays quadratic.  MonPoly's windowed replay would cover it, and the other formulas where no
conjunct can be hoisted (q under a negation or in the left operand of a Since).  But it would
change the FRZ node at all three levels and its invariant: the stored state would have to be
the ψ-state at the window's edge rather than at time point 0.

The outputs are identical on the `frz` family and on the 652 `-verified` commands of the test
corpus.  The cram tests pass.

## 21.48 Windowed replay for FRZ

`frz-lagphi` was the case 21.47 left open: when the frozen formula lags, the FRZ node replays
the body once per frozen table, and each replay started from the state at time point 0 and fed
it the whole prefix, so the node cost O(j) per frozen time point and the run was quadratic.
MonPoly replays only a window: a time point whose timestamp lies more than the body's past
reach before the first undecided one can never influence a verdict still to be computed.
The verified monitor now does the same, at all three levels (kernel, dense, unboxed).

**The locality lemma** (`Frz_Locality.thy`, new, before `Monitor`).  `past_reach φ :: enat` is
the furthest a formula looks back in time: 0 for predicates and the future operators (for
their own interval; their operands count), the upper bound of the interval plus the operand's
reach for Prev, Since, Trigger and MatchP, the sum along a `LET`, and ∞ for `LETPAST` and an
unbounded past interval.  `sat_agree_from`: if two valuations agree on every time point whose
timestamp is at most `past_reach φ` before that of i, then φ has the same truth value at i
under both.  The proof is by induction on φ; the only non-trivial cases are Let (reaches add
up) and the regular expressions (`match_cong_between`).  `ivl_ub` computes an interval's upper
bound by a linear search for the first difference outside it (`while_option`), once per
operator at initialisation.

**The node.**  `MFrz` gains a *replay base* ρ (a second copy of the body's state) and its window
data: `w` (the replay base has absorbed time points before w with the frozen predicate empty),
`pR` (its progress), `tq` (the timestamps from w on), `rb` (its verdicts from the first undecided
time point on) and `d = past_reach ψ`.  Per step:
1. The body state with the frozen predicate empty (`zs0`) is unchanged; it decides the time
   points that do not depend on the frozen predicate.
2. The replay base absorbs the new time points with the frozen predicate empty.
3. Each frozen table α is replayed from the replay base, feeding α over the window
   `[w, j)` only (was: from the time-0 state over `[0, j)`); the verdict for α's own time point
   is read from `rb` or from the replay's output.
4. The window moves forward over the time points whose timestamp plus d lies before that of the
   first undecided time point (`frz_adv`); the replay base absorbs them with empty tables.

With d = ∞ the window never moves and the node behaves as before (with one extra state).

**The invariant** (`wf_frz_win`): the replay base is well formed for every frozen value i and
every w' ≥ w under the valuation "empty before w', φ at i after"; the time points before w lie
beyond the reach seen from the first undecided time point; `rb` holds the freeze's verdicts on
`[min jfrz pR, pR)`.  `qtable_Frz_window` turns a replay verdict into the freeze's verdict via
the locality lemma.  The MFrz case of `meval_correct` was rewritten around `progress_Frz_eq`,
a closed form of the freeze's progress (the `LEAST` in `frz_progress` is one of three values),
and is shorter than before (about 460 lines instead of 1190).  The dense layers follow by
simulation (`DsFrz` relates the two replay bases and `rb` modulo the dense table view).

| formula | before (s) | after (s) | MonPoly (s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| frz-lagphi | 2.36 | 0.15 | 0.12 |
| frz-neglag | 7.52 | 0.35 | 0.13 |
| frz-sincelag | 8.30 | 0.39 | 0.13 |

`frz-neglag` (the frozen predicate under a negation) and `frz-sincelag` (in the left operand of a
Since) are new in the suite: no conjunct can be hoisted there, so 21.47 did not help them.  The
other formulas of the `frz` family are unchanged within noise; `frz-onceinf` (d = ∞) pays for
the extra state (0.79 → 0.87 s).  The outputs are identical to the previous binary and to
MonPoly on the `frz` family and on nine further FRZ shapes (negation, Since left operand, Prev,
unbounded Once, a future window, a lower bound, nested freezes, an aggregation), and on the 652
`-verified` commands of the test corpus.  The cram tests pass.

## 21.49 The window also moves when the frozen formula does not lag

The formulas the rewriter produces (`Rewriting.thy`, rules 2, 3, 10–13 and 18–21) freeze a guard
α and conjoin its frozen predicate q with an operand β under the temporal operator, for example
`C(x) AND ONCE[0,5] NOT B(x,x)` becomes `FRZ q(x) = C(x) IN C(x) AND ONCE[0,5] (q(x) AND NOT
B(x,x))`.  The rewriter only does this when β has a free variable that α restricts and β does not
(`prop_cond`), so taking q out of the operator again leaves it unsafe, and the hoisting of 21.43
and 21.47 never applies to these formulas.  They were quadratic after 21.48: the window moved only
while some verdict was still undecided (`jfrz < j`).  When the frozen formula keeps up, every
verdict is out after each step (`jfrz = j`), so the window stayed at 0 and each copy replayed the
whole prefix.

`frz_adv` now moves the window whenever it is not empty.  It uses the timestamp of `min jfrz (j - 1)`:
that of the first undecided time point, or, once all are decided, that of the last one.  The
latter is no greater than the timestamp of any time point still to be decided, so the invariant
of 21.48 (`∀k < w. τ k + d < τ jfrz`) is unchanged, and only the step lemma's `a_facts` changes
(twelve lines of `Correctness.thy`).  Every other proof, including the dense layers, is untouched.

| formula (tp1000) | before (s) | after (s) | MonPoly (s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `C(x) AND ONCE[0,5] NOT B(x,x)` (`frzrw-once`) | 3.73 | 0.11 | 0.11 |
| `C(x) AND ONCE[0,20] NOT B(x,x)` (`frzrw-once20`) | 2.55 | 0.15 | 0.13 |
| `FRZ q(x) = C(x) IN (C(x) AND ONCE[0,5] (q(x) AND NOT B(x,x)))` | 2.66 | 0.10 | 0.16 |
| `FRZ q(x) = C(x) IN ONCE[0,5] (q(x) AND NOT B(x,x))` | 1.65 | 0.11 | 0.12 |

The `frz` family of the suite is unchanged within noise.  All of its formulas are either hoisted
or lagging, which is why the suite did not show the problem.  A new group, `frz_rw`, has eight
formulas that the rewriter freezes (Once with and without a lower bound, a binary guard, the right
operand of Since with and without a lower bound, Eventually, the right operand of Until); two more,
into a Trigger and a Release, are in `vm_only`.  None of them is monitorable without the rewrite.
Now linear, they run on the full `base` log (6000 time points), where the verified monitor is
within 0.8–1.7× of MonPoly with the same verdicts; the previous binary takes more than the
timeout on most of them (on `tp1000` already: 19 s for `A(x,y) AND ONCE[0,10] NOT B(x,y)`,
11 s for `frzrw-sincelb`, against 0.37 and 0.27 s now).  An unbounded window
(`C(x) AND ONCE[0,*) NOT B(x,x)`, d = ∞) stays quadratic; MonPoly refuses it.  After the change, a profile of `frz-rwonce20` puts 44% of the
instructions in the copies' evaluation (their Since windows and joins) and 31% in reading the
log.  The frozen predicate's table (`replicate`) does not show up.  A constant result for the
frozen predicate (the `xres` of the `const-relation-propagation` branch) would therefore save
little on these shapes.

The outputs are identical to the previous binary on the 652 `-verified` commands of the test
corpus, the verdicts agree with MonPoly on the `frz` and `frz_rw` families, and the cram tests
pass.  The exported code differs from the previous export in `frz_adv` alone.

## 21.50 The copies stop at the last pending verdict

When the frozen formula lags (`frz-neglag`, `frz-sincelag`), each of the copies that
decides the pending time points replays the window up to the current time point `j`.  But the
copy for the frozen tuples of time point k only has to produce the verdicts from k up to the
last time point whose frozen tuples have arrived (`k + length xs` in `frz_cut`).  If the frozen
predicate is used only in the past or at the current time point, the later time points
cannot change these verdicts.  That is exactly LetPast's notion `safe_letpast (q, nfv φ) ψ ≤
NonFutuRec`.  `MFrz` now carries this flag (`po`, computed once by `minit0`), and the copies
are fed `frz_cut po w jphi (length xs) j` time points instead of `j - w`.

The proof reuses `safe_letpast_progress_upd'` (Progress.thy): with the flag, the body's
progress with q defined up to the cut is at least that with q defined up to `j`, as far as
the pending verdicts are concerned.  The step lemma of `Correctness.thy` changes where the
copies are built (thirty lines); the dense layers follow by simulation.

| formula (tp500) | before (s) | after (s) | MonPoly (s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `frz-neglag` | 0.40 | 0.31 | 0.16 |
| `frz-sincelag` | 0.39 | 0.26 | 0.16 |
| `frz-neglag30` (lag 30) | 0.52 | 0.29 | 0.10 |
| `frz-sincelag30` (lag 30) | 2.90 | 0.52 | 0.16 |

The two lag-30 formulas are new in the `frz` group.  The rest of `frz` and `frz_rw` is
unchanged within noise, and the verdicts agree with MonPoly on both groups.  On the 652
`-verified` commands of the test corpus, the outputs are identical to the previous binary.
The cram tests pass.

The flag exposed a gap in the code setup: the code equations `safe_letpast_code` in
`Monitor_Impl.thy` had no cases for `Frz`, `Trigger` and `Release`.  So far the function was
applied only to LetPast bodies.  Applied to a freeze whose body holds a nested freeze
(`frz42`) or a trigger (the rewriter's freeze into a trigger), the exported code failed with a
match failure.  The three equations are now there.

### The FRZ equations, laid out for the function package

The function package took about three minutes on each of `meval`, `meeval`, `deval`,
`edeval` and `udeval`, and nearly all of it went into their FRZ equation.
`pat_completeness` and the compatibility proof take a few seconds; the rest is the
package's own work after the proof.  In a scratch copy of `udeval` with the FRZ equation
alone (plus a catch-all), the definition took 186 s.  Bisecting the equation showed that the
cost comes from the chain of `let`s after a recursive call's binding.  The same chain before
the call costs almost nothing (9 s with the copy call last, over 180 s with seven more
`let`s after it).  The package's congruence steps (`split_cong`, `let_cong`) unify over that
whole remaining chain.

Each stage now computes the values it needs inside the bound term of its call and returns
them as a tuple.  Only the pair `let`s of the φ, ψ and ρ calls, the copies' stage and the
advance's stage remain at the top level:

    (zs0_out, jfrz, rb0, rb, dbe, ys, αs') =
      (let zs0_out = ...; ...; (ys, αs') = freeze_meval0 (...) (jfrz - rb0) αs
       in (zs0_out, jfrz + length ys, rb0, rb, dbe, ys, αs'));
    (a, tq, rs2, ρ) = (let tq = ...; a = frz_adv ...; (rs2, ρ) = ... in (a, tq, rs2, ρ))

The computation is the same.  The scratch definition now takes 9 s instead of 186 s.
`Monitor.thy`'s whole `meval` block, with its termination proof, checks in 19 s, and the
block of each dense layer in 20–30 s.  `MFODL_Monitor_Dense` builds in 11 minutes.
Correctness, `DsFrz` and Dense_Let go through unchanged.  The termination cases and the three
`Rep_…` simulations only needed their premise lists adjusted; the simulations also needed a
lemma that evaluates the copies' stage to its tuple.

## 21.51 The live copy

MonPoly monitors a freeze whose body looks into the future (`frzrw-untilr`, `frzrw-ev`: the
rewriter's guard frozen into an Until or an Eventually) with one instance of the body per
pending anchor.  Each instance is fed the new time points as they arrive.  VeriMon instead
rebuilt the copy for the first pending anchor jfrz at every step.  It replayed the replay base
over the whole window, with the frozen predicate holding jfrz's tuples.  The copies stop at
the first anchor whose verdict is not out yet, so only that anchor's copy is ever carried from
one step to the next.

`MFrz` now keeps that copy (the live copy) in a slot of its own, `ρL`, next to `lv`, the number
of its verdicts still to come before jfrz's.  At the next step `frz_live_step` resumes it: if no
verdict of the body without the frozen predicate is new and a frozen table is pending
(`zs0_out = [] ∧ αs ≠ []`), the live copy is fed the new time points with the frozen predicate
holding the first pending table.  It is not replayed from the replay base.  When its verdict
for jfrz is out, the remaining anchors are copied as before (`freeze_meval1` is
`freeze_meval0` that also returns the state of the copy it stopped at).  Otherwise it stays the
live copy.  With `po` (a past-only body, §21.50) the copies are cut short and no live copy is
kept, so those formulas run as before.

*Termination.*  The node's size must not change under evaluation, since the parent evaluates
the replay base's result again.  A new live copy has the size of the replay base, but the slot
may hold anything.  So `frz_keep_lv` and `frz_keep_st` replace the slot only when the sizes
agree, which they always do in a monitor state.  The unboxed layer's Rep map does not keep the
size: a guarded Since node stands for two boxed nodes.  The unboxed evaluator therefore
compares, and terminates by, `ursize`, the size of the boxed formula computed on the short one.
The same holds for the size guard of its recursive let (`uletpast_meval0`).  So both guards
take the boxed evaluator's branches on every formula, and `udeval_Rep` stays unconditional.

*Correctness.*  The Frz rule of `wf_mformula` gets one premise.  If `lv = Some g`, then `po`
is false and, for some wL before which every time point is out of the body's reach from jfrz,
`ρL` is well formed at `j` with the frozen predicate defined up to `j` and holding jfrz's
tuples from wL on, and `jfrz` is `g` past its progress.  Without `po` the body's progress
under the live copy is exactly that of a fresh copy (`jpsic = jpsij'`).  So the live copy has
its verdict exactly when a fresh one would (`frz_live_step_spec`), and the verdict is right by
the same argument as a fresh copy's (`qtable_Frz_window`).  The step's remaining verdicts are
the fresh copies' ones, unchanged.  The gap `g` turns out to be 0 whenever it is kept.
`Dense_Eval`'s simulation carries `size ρL = size ρ` on both sides and relates the live copies
when `lv ≠ None`.  `produces` and `mok` carry the live copy under the same condition.

| formula | before (s) | after (s) | MonPoly (s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `frzrw-untilr` | 1.16 | 0.92 | 0.73 |
| `frzrw-ev` | 0.82 | 0.73 | 0.74 |

(minimum of 7 interleaved runs; MonPoly from the suite run.)  The rest of `frz` and `frz_rw`
is unchanged within noise, and the verdicts agree with MonPoly on both groups.  On the 652
`-verified` commands of the test corpus, the outputs are identical to the previous binary.
The cram tests pass.  `MFODL_Monitor_Devel` builds in 27 minutes and `MFODL_Monitor_Dense` in
11.

## 21.52 The Since join walks the index

After 21.51 the two worst ratios of the suite were `frz-sincelag30` (2.79) and `frz-neglag30`
(2.13).  In `frz-sincelag30` (`FRZ q(x) = EVENTUALLY[0,30] C(x) IN (q(x) SINCE[0,4] A(x,y))`)
each copy of the body joins its Since node's state with the frozen table q at every step.  Since q
binds only one of the node's two variables, the join goes through the node's indices, which cluster
the stored tuples by the value of x.  With a lag of 30, q holds about three quarters of the 200
values of x, while a copy's index holds the x-values of about five time points.
`mfilter_keys_RBT_code` computed the part of each index to keep by looking up every tuple of q and
inserting it into a new tree.  It computed the part to drop by deleting every tuple of q from a
copy of the index.  That is four passes over q per join, two for each of the two indices, however
few keys actually drop out.  A profile put 48% of the instructions in this join.  From lag 3 to
lag 30 the lookups and deletions grew fivefold, from 223k and 219k to 1.15M and 1.12M, for 1.3
times the joins.  MonPoly's join (`idx_table_inv_semijoin`) walks the index instead and removes
only the buckets whose key fails the probe.

The join now does the same for a positive left operand (`gjoin_idx`, `gjoin_idx1` in
`Dense_Idx_Code.thy`).  One pass over the index's entries collects those whose key is not in q.
Their keys are deleted from the index, and their buckets from the window's table and the time
stamps.  The two operations are stated as `mfilter_keys` and `del_buckets` of their definitions,
with code equations on the tree (`lookup_fold_delete_fst`, `set_of_idx_mfilter_keys_False_RBT`).
`gjoin_msaux_code` calls them.  The verified algorithm is unchanged.  Rebuilding the kept part
from the filtered entries (`RBT_Mapping2.filter`, linear in the index) was measured too: 0.32 s
against 0.27 s on `frz-sincelag30`, since few keys drop.  A negated left operand keeps the old
equations, which visit only the left table: there the left table is typically the smaller one.

| formula (tp500, min of 7) | before (s) | after (s) | MonPoly (s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `frz-sincelag30` | 0.377 | 0.269 | 0.131 |
| `frz-sincelag` | 0.179 | 0.174 | 0.098 |

Over the 133 suite formulas with Since, Once, Trigger, a freeze or a policy, timed against the
previous binary, the geometric mean of new over old is 0.99.  The largest apparent slowdowns
were 1.19 (`since-nb5`) and 1.16 (`once-raw5`); timed again with 9 interleaved runs they are 1.00
and 0.95.  The outputs are identical on those formulas and on the 652 `-verified` commands of the
test corpus.  The benchmark formulas agree with MonPoly, and the cram tests pass.

## 21.53 The replay base advances with a frozen table under a negation

`frz-neglag30` (`FRZ q(x) = EVENTUALLY[0,30] C(x) IN ONCE[0,5] (A(x,y) AND NOT q(x))`) was the other
of the two worst ratios after 21.51.  The FRZ copies start from the replay base.  The replay base holds the
buffered input of the time points from the window's start w on.  When the window moves, it absorbs the time points that leave it, with some
value of the frozen predicate.  Until now that value was the empty table.  Under a negation,
`A(x,y) AND NOT q(x)` with q empty is all of A, so the replay base's Once window held every A tuple
of the last five time points.  Each copy then expired those tuples one by one: a profile showed
165k window deletions against 75k insertions, and 43% of the instructions in `de_gsw_expire`.
MonPoly starts each copy empty and injects only the window's history.

Which value the replay base absorbs does not matter for the verdicts: those time points lie beyond
the body's past reach from every pending verdict.  So the node now takes the latest frozen table it
knows when the frozen predicate occurs under a negation (`neg_pred`, a new flag `ng` of `MFrz`,
computed once by `minit0`), and the empty table otherwise (`frz_fill ng (αs @ xs)`).  Consecutive
frozen tables of a lagging formula overlap heavily, so the replay base's window then holds about what
a copy keeps itself.  For a positive occurrence (`frz-sincelag30`, the left operand of a Since), the
empty table leaves the least, and a prototype that took the latest table there slowed it from 0.37 to
0.53 s.

*Correctness.*  The replay base's invariant said that it is well formed with the frozen predicate
empty before w and equal to the frozen formula's value from some point on.  It now says: for some fixed
N, and for every M, the replay base is well formed with the frozen predicate given, at each time
point k, by the frozen formula's value at the time point `(if k < w then N k else M k)`, or empty
where that is `None` (`frz_val`).  The prefix N records what the replay base absorbed.  M covers
everything from w on, so a copy for the frozen table of i picks `M = (λ_. Some i)` (`frz_cv`), and
the advance picks the fill's time point on `[w, w + a)`.  `frz_fill_val` shows that the fill is the
value of the frozen formula at some time point, or empty.  `qtable_Frz_window` now takes any
valuation that agrees with the frozen value from w on.  The live copy's invariant carries its own
prefix in the same way.  In the step lemma, the changes are local, and so are they in the transfer
lemma of `Sat.thy`.  The dense layers only thread the flag and the fill through
(`frz_fill_cases` for their table invariants, `frz_fill_reopt` for the unboxed one).

| formula (tp500, min of 5) | before (s) | after (s) | MonPoly (s) |
|---|---|---|---|
| `frz-neglag30` | 0.186 | 0.112 | 0.078 |
| `frz-neglag` | 0.231 | 0.197 | 0.115 |

Over the 37 formulas with a freeze in the suite, the geometric mean of new over old is 0.975.  Only
the two formulas above have the frozen predicate under a negation.  The others run the same code, and
their spread (0.85–1.10, on runs of 0.02–0.3 s timed while a PIDE session was running) is noise.
The outputs are identical on all 37 formulas, on the 652 `-verified` commands of the test corpus, and
on the benchmark formulas, which agree with MonPoly.  The cram tests pass.
